For fixed P=13, Z_{13,K}(3)/Z_{13,K}(2)→0.7425170401859808513545707238034415975921, while the prefactor grows as exp(Kθ(13)/2). This is consistent with, and illustrates, the exact ratio proof; the finite table itself is not the proof.

### 5. Source audit

Positivity of the uncentered trace comes from a finite diagonal sum of positive exponentials. Reflection comes only from the finite-box complement and its centering by C_{P,K}/2. The divergence is forced by the exact ratio identity and θ(P)→∞; it uses no zero information and no target positivity. The numerical check is a direct evaluation of the specified finite products. No assumption equivalent to the target is used.

### 6. Discrimination test

| Toy world | Result | Exact step |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Curves over finite fields | **Artificial analogue only** | A finite product over closed-point norms has the same box-complement ratio obstruction, but the construction contains no Frobenius cohomology or curve-zeta numerator. |
| (b) Ihara zeta of Ramanujan graphs | **Artificial analogue only** | Replacing prime labels by primitive-cycle norms preserves the box formula; no nonbacktracking operator or Ramanujan spectral bound appears. |
| (c) Random Euler products with independent phases | **Fails phase sensitivity** | The occupation energies use only positive norms, so changing independent local phases leaves F_{P,K} and the obstruction unchanged. |
| (d) Davenport–Heilbronn | **Unavailable canonically** | There is no Euler-product prime set for this function; assigning an unrelated set of norms makes an unrelated box trace. |
| (e) Beurling systems with bad zeros | **Fails discrimination** | For any generalized-prime norms q>1, the finite geometric boxes, complement identity, and divergent centered ratio hold verbatim, irrespective of the associated zeta zeros. |

### 7. Verdict

**DEAD.** Candidate 23A has an exact scoped no-go: scalar normalization cannot make the centered finite-box traces converge to a nonzero analytic function on a domain containing an interval of positive real parameters. Candidates 23B and 23C fail respectively because infinite coordinate caps have divergent total degree and because deleting the centering deletes the finite reflection. The construction also fails the required false-world discrimination: its identities persist for generalized-prime systems with bad zeros.

### 8. New-idea check

The finite box complement and its exact symmetry are Pass 22A. The present joint P,K ratio theorem and scalar-normalization obstruction are a new, narrower limit diagnostic in this ledger. The Euler-product convergence for Re z>1, the complement identity, and the analytic identity theorem are standard; no new theorem about the target is claimed.

### 9. Lesson

When a finite cutoff symmetry is produced by centering at a cutoff-dependent top energy, test ratios at two fixed parameters before searching for scalar renormalization: the scalar cancels, exposing whether the symmetry can coexist with a nonzero limit.

### 10. Seed

Can a genuinely operator-valued renormalization (rather than a scalar factor) remove the diverging complement energy while preserving a positive trace and an arithmetic-sensitive reflection, and can its exact analogue fail for bad Beurling systems without using their zero locations? Next operator: **CHANGE-THE-OBJECT**.

## Pass 24 — Second cumulant of the centered occupation trace

### 1. Operator used

**CHANGE-THE-OBJECT.** Replace the divergent centered trace itself by its second logarithmic derivative. This removes the cutoff-dependent affine term from the logarithm while preserving the finite even symmetry. The test is whether the resulting positive variance has the correct arithmetic parameter and distinguishes the required true and false worlds.

### 2. Generate three candidates

**24A — First centered cumulant.** For the capped prime occupation trace F_{P,K}(z), define A_{P,K}(z)=d/dz log F_{P,K}(z)−Kθ(P)/2. Claim: A has a finite limit equal to the Euler logarithmic derivative and retains a useful reflection law.

**24B — Second centered cumulant.** Define V_{P,K}(z)=d²/dz² log F_{P,K}(z). Claim: V is even, nonnegative on the real axis as a Gibbs-energy variance, and converges in Re z>1 to (log ζ)''(z), whose meromorphic continuation exposes the arithmetic singularities.

**24C — Double primitive as a renormalized determinant.** Integrate V_{P,K} twice with fixed basepoint conditions to define a normalized logarithmic determinant G_{P,K}. Claim: the second derivative removes the divergent center energy while the basepoint data preserve the finite reflection and produce a nonzero determinant limit.

### 3. Fast kill

- **24A:** Kill: subtracting Kθ(P)/2 leaves d log Z_{P,K}/dz, which converges to ζ'/ζ in Re z>1, but its finite reflection law is lost; the unsubtracted derivative is odd and diverges by Kθ(P)/2.
- **24B:** Keep for full audit: its variance positivity and evenness are exact, so test the limiting parameter against the required completed arithmetic object and run the false-world check.
- **24C:** Kill: integrating V recovers log F only up to an affine function; choosing the finite-part slope that removes Kθ(P)/2 is exactly the uncentering operation, while retaining the centered slope leaves the Pass 23 divergence.

### 4. Develop 24B: exact variance and limit

For S_P={p:p≤P}, cap K, and occupation vector a with 0≤a_p≤K, let E(a)=Σ_p a_p log p and C_{P,K}=Kθ(P). Define
\[
F_{P,K}(z)=\sum_a e^{-z(E(a)-C_{P,K}/2)}
=e^{zC_{P,K}/2}\prod_{p≤P}\sum_{j=0}^K p^{-jz}.
\]
Complementation a↦K−a makes F_{P,K}(z)=F_{P,K}(−z). For real z, the normalized weights proportional to e^{-zE(a)} are a product of truncated geometric laws. Differentiating the finite sum gives
\[
V_{P,K}(z)=(\log F_{P,K})''(z)
=\sum_{p≤P}(\log p)^2\operatorname{Var}_{p,z,K}(a)\ge0.
\]
Since log F is even on the real axis, V_{P,K} is even there as well. The variance identity is exact for every finite P,K.

For a single prime write q=p^{-z}. Its local variance is
\[
\operatorname{Var}_{p,z,K}(a)
=\frac{\sum_{j=0}^K j^2q^j}{\sum_{j=0}^Kq^j}
-\left(\frac{\sum_{j=0}^K jq^j}{\sum_{j=0}^Kq^j}\right)^2.
\]
For fixed real z>1, first letting K grow and then P grow, or taking any joint exhaustion, gives
\[
V_{P,K}(z)\longrightarrow
\sum_p(\log p)^2\frac{p^{-z}}{(1-p^{-z})^2}
=(\log\zeta)''(z)
=\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{\Lambda(n)\log n}{n^z}.
\]
The last equality follows by expanding each absolutely convergent geometric series. It is an ordinary Euler-product derivative in its convergence half-plane.

**Parameter mismatch.** The finite reflection uses z↦−z. To place that reflection at the target center, one must set z=s−1/2; the limit then contains ζ(s−1/2), not ζ(s). If instead z=s so the limit contains ζ(s), the finite reflection is s↦−s, not s↦1−s. The second derivative removes a linear centering constant but cannot change this half-step in the Euler exponent. Completing by gamma and pole factors does not repair the mismatch in the prime factor. Thus V has no proved canonical identification with the required completed determinant.

**Numerical check.** At z=2, the following values were computed with 60-digit arithmetic from the finite local variance formula; the K=∞ column is the exact geometric-local limit for that finite prime set.

| prime cutoff P | K=1 | K=2 | K=4 | K=8 | K=∞ |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 2 | 0.0768724822269122279467364042122663954768885 | 0.143809065390482059084030688152199039157444 | 0.201781919554435245440292214340167444031555 | 0.213386216313227758438192974276155838442784 | 0.213534672852533966518712233922962209658024 |
| 5 | 0.281292651791029626114665098028556032413810 | 0.409528294796193457992160013002541842801025 | 0.483417272400064420356644682037400816743479 | 0.495538959879286191419595993503991121313138 | 0.495687668816639893315327484194863271095362 |
| 13 | 0.440725528753979994143316631620177280780539 | 0.577436169262982295006425532818624465793606 | 0.651655955585869328935670680124022310473163 | 0.663777984923785168585571942325121619519330 | 0.663926693861327309092108333285592837567610 |

Directly evaluating at z=−2 returns the same values, as required by the exact evenness. For P=13,K=∞, extending the prime sum through 200,000 gives 0.884415863905505315173493084174362919025005; this is a finite partial sum, not an infinite-tail enclosure.

### 5. Source audit

The sign comes solely from variance of a finite Gibbs distribution. The evenness comes solely from the box complement. The limit and Λ(n)log(n) coefficients come from differentiating the ordinary Euler product. All are unconditional identities, but none supplies a sign for the complete Weil form or a zero-sensitive estimate. No target-equivalent assumption was used.

### 6. Discrimination test

| Toy world | Result | Exact step |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Curves over finite fields | **No curve spectral identification** | Replacing primes by closed-point norms gives a positive local occupation variance, but it does not recover Frobenius eigenvalues or the curve-zeta numerator. |
| (b) Ihara zeta of Ramanujan graphs | **No graph spectral identification** | Primitive-cycle weights can define the same formal variance, but the construction contains no adjacency/nonbacktracking operator and proves no Ramanujan bound. |
| (c) Random Euler products with independent phases | **Phase-blind** | V depends only on positive local norms and is unchanged by varying independent phases; it does not encode the phase-dependent zero data. |
| (d) Davenport–Heilbronn | **Unavailable canonically** | Without an Euler product there is no canonical occupation monoid; an externally assigned norm set produces an unrelated variance. |
| (e) Beurling systems with bad zeros | **Fails discrimination** | Replacing p by any generalized-prime norm q>1 gives the same finite variance proof and convergence in the Euler-product half-plane, irrespective of off-line zeros. |

### 7. Verdict

**DEAD.** The second cumulant is a finite positive even observable, but its arithmetic limit is centered at the wrong exponent if the symmetry is placed at the target center. Even ignoring that shift, its variance proof is unchanged for false Beurling systems and therefore cannot discriminate the zero geometry. Candidates 24A and 24C fail because removing the divergent affine slope removes the finite reflection, while retaining it reproduces the divergent centered trace.

### 8. New-idea check

The identity between a log-partition second derivative and a Gibbs variance is standard, as are derivatives of Euler products and the resulting Λ(n)log(n) series. The finite cap implementation is a scoped synthesis of Passes 22–23, not a new theorem or a new spectral realization. The half-step mismatch is an exact diagnostic for this centered occupation family; it is not a no-go for other operator families.

### 9. Lesson

Differentiating away a divergent scalar can preserve positivity, but the parameter of the surviving Euler product must still match the center of the desired reflection; test that exponent before interpreting cumulant positivity as arithmetic spectral structure.

### 10. Seed

Can an imported transfer-operator construction place the half-shift in its local weights while making the duality act on the same parameter, rather than shifting the Euler product after the fact? Test any such operator first on a false Beurling system. Next operator: **IMPORT**.
## Pass 25 — Prime-suspension transfer and the reflected trace-class test

### 1. Operator used

**IMPORT.** Import the primitive-orbit transfer construction from dynamical zeta functions: an orbit of length \(\log p\) receives weight \(p^{-s}\), and the determinant of the transfer operator should produce the Euler product. Test whether this standard mechanism can retain its positive trace, acquire the reflection \(s\leftrightarrow1-s\), and remain defined on a common domain.

### 2. Generate three candidates

**25A — Diagonal prime suspension.** On \(\mathcal H=\ell^2(\mathcal P)\), define \(L_s e_p=p^{-s}e_p\). Claim: its Fredholm determinant recovers the Euler product exactly, while an arithmetic involution on the prime states makes the same family reflection-symmetric and exposes the completed zeros.

**25B — Reflected two-channel transfer.** Set \(\mathbb L_s=L_s\oplus L_{1-s}\) and let \(J\) swap its two channels. Claim: this gives an intrinsically positive, reflection-equivariant transfer family whose determinant is defined in a neighborhood of the critical line.

**25C — Full shift on prime symbols.** Use the full symbolic shift with one letter for each prime and weight \(p^{-s}\); its Ruelle zeta is \((1-\sum_p p^{-s})^{-1}\). Claim: periodic words recover the commutative Euler product while the shift supplies a canonical dynamical spectrum.

### 3. Fast kill

- **25A:** Keep for exact determinant and trace-class-domain checks; the determinant is a genuine Euler identity in its convergence half-plane.
- **25B:** Kill: trace-class convergence of the first block requires \(\operatorname{Re}s>1\), while that of the reflected block requires \(\operatorname{Re}s<0\); the required common domain is empty.
- **25C:** Kill: the ordered words \((2,3)\) and \((3,2)\) both contribute to the integer 6, giving coefficient 2 instead of the Euler-product coefficient 1.

### 4. Develop 25A: exact determinant, wrong global domain

Let \(\mathcal H=\ell^2(\mathcal P)\), with orthonormal basis \(e_p\), and define
\[
L_s e_p=p^{-s}e_p.
\]
For \(\sigma=\operatorname{Re}s>1\), \(L_s\) is trace class because \(\|L_s\|_1=\sum_p p^{-\sigma}<\infty\). Its Fredholm determinant is
\[
\det(I-L_s)^{-1}=\prod_p(1-p^{-s})^{-1}=\zeta(s).
\]
The equality follows first for finite prime cutoffs and then by absolute convergence. Equivalently,
\[
\log\det(I-L_s)^{-1}=\sum_{m\ge1}\frac{\operatorname{Tr}(L_s^m)}m=\sum_p\sum_{m\ge1}\frac{p^{-ms}}m,
\]
and logarithmic differentiation yields
\[
-\frac{d}{ds}\log\det(I-L_s)^{-1}=\sum_p\sum_{m\ge1}(\log p)p^{-ms}=-\frac{\zeta'(s)}{\zeta(s)}.
\]
For real \(s>1\), \(L_s\) is positive and trace class. These are exact arithmetic facts, but the actual eigenvalues of \(L_s\) are \(p^{-s}\); no Xi-zero ordinates appear as spectral data.

The reflection fails at the operator level. For real \(s>1\), \(L_s\) is bounded with norm \(2^{-s}<1\), whereas \(L_{1-s}\) has diagonal entries \(p^{s-1}\) and is unbounded. Thus no unitary conjugacy between these two operators exists on \(\ell^2(\mathcal P)\). Adding the same gamma and pole factors to the scalar determinant would be an external completion, not a consequence of the imported transfer operator.

The trace-class obstruction persists at the critical line. At \(\sigma=1/2\),
\[
\|L_{1/2}\|_1=\sum_p p^{-1/2}=\infty,\qquad \|L_{1/2}\|_2^2=\sum_p p^{-1}=\infty.
\]
Hence neither the ordinary Fredholm determinant nor the standard Hilbert–Schmidt regularized determinant \(\det_2\) is supplied there by this operator. For \(\sigma>1/2\), \(L_s\) is Hilbert–Schmidt, but \(\det_2\) removes the linear prime term; its convergence domain does not include the boundary \(\sigma=1/2\).

A finite Euler determinant already shows the missing reflection. For \(I=\{2,3\}\), put \(D_I(s)=(1-2^{-s})(1-3^{-s})\). At 70-digit working precision:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
s&D_I(s)\\\hline
0.4&0.08610704369813363342296490451182756848872745301401567933\\
0.6&0.164242938488302861590690195267717138311853259666183082
\end{array}
\]
and
\[
D_I(0.4)/D_I(0.6)=0.5242663367488767215843397662516805912269941379182769599\ne1.
\]
Thus the finite prime factor itself is not invariant under \(s\mapsto1-s\). This is a diagnostic of the local factor alone; it does not deny that the completed zeta function has a functional equation.

**Requested finite-place Gram check.** The archive's Pass 15A defines the separate Brownian log-place Gram
\[
K^{\rm Br}_{S,I}(p,q)=(pq)^{-1/4}\min(\log p,\log q),\qquad p,q\in I,
\]
with \(\infty\) a boundary label and not an additional matrix row. Recomputing its largest eigenvalues at 60-digit precision gives:

| \(S\) | \(\lambda_{\max}(K^{\rm Br}_{S,I})\) | \(1-\lambda_{\max}\) |
|---|---:|---:|
| \(\{\infty,2\}\) | 0.49012907173427359585695086181761669064573034954953 | 0.50987092826572640414304913818238330935426965045047 |
| \(\{\infty,2,3\}\) | 1.0109150546250736071479608970571510630871205574923 | -0.010915054625073607147960897057151063087120557492256 |
| \(\{\infty,2,3,5\}\) | 1.5597157287383607901847746406579447735543995900544 | -0.55971572873836079018477464065794477355439959005439 |

So the contraction condition first fails when 3 is added; it does not remain below 1 with margin. This is a recheck of Pass 15A's Brownian Gram, not evidence about the distinct diagonal operator \(L_s\), and no larger-set sweep is warranted under the stated below-1 stopping rule.

### 5. Source audit

The determinant and logarithmic derivative are the standard Euler-product/Fredholm-determinant identities in \(\operatorname{Re}s>1\). Trace-class criteria follow from the prime series; the Hilbert–Schmidt boundary test uses divergence of \(\sum_p1/p\). The lack of reflection is witnessed by exact operator boundedness and by the finite determinant comparison. The Brownian matrix values use its explicit Gram formula and high-precision symmetric eigensolver. No zero locations are inputs, and no Xi identity or positivity of the complete Weil form is proved.

### 6. Discrimination test

| Toy world | Result | Exact step |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Curves over finite fields | **Euler analogue only** | Replacing primes by closed-point norms gives a positive Euler transfer in its convergence domain, but this diagonal operator contains no Frobenius action or cohomological determinant and proves no Weil bound. |
| (b) Ihara zeta of Ramanujan graphs | **Euler analogue only** | Primitive-cycle weights can form a dynamical zeta, but this construction has no Hashimoto/nonbacktracking adjacency operator and yields no Ramanujan spectral bound. |
| (c) Random Euler products with independent phases | **Fails phase selectivity** | The positive diagonal uses only \(|p^{-s}|\); inserting independent phases as eigenvalues abandons the positive real diagonal mechanism, while taking moduli erases the phase data. |
| (d) Davenport–Heilbronn | **Unavailable canonically** | Without a canonical Euler product there is no prime-indexed diagonal transfer prescribed by the function; choosing one externally does not represent its determinant. |
| (e) Beurling systems with bad zeros | **Fails discrimination** | Replacing primes by generalized-prime norms \(q\) gives the same trace-class identity \(\operatorname{Tr}(L_s)=\sum_q q^{-s}\) and Euler determinant in the convergence half-plane, regardless of off-line zeros. |

### 7. Verdict

**DEAD.** Candidate 25A gives the exact Euler determinant and positive trace only for \(\operatorname{Re}s>1\); its reflected operator is unbounded there, and the center is outside both trace-class and Hilbert–Schmidt determinant domains. The finite local determinant is not reflection invariant, and the positive construction survives unchanged in bad-zero generalized-prime systems. Candidate 25B formalizes the reflection but has no common determinant domain; 25C loses commutative Euler multiplicities.

### 8. New-idea check

The prime suspension is the standard dynamical-zeta/Fredholm determinant construction, and the bosonic prime trace is already recorded in Pass 19. The trace-class abscissa, mixed-word coefficient, and two-prime reflection check are elementary audits of this imported construction, not a new operator theorem. The Brownian Gram definition and its original values are in Pass 15A; this pass rechecks the user-requested three cutoffs and does not claim novelty.

### 9. Lesson

An imported transfer determinant must have a trace or determinant domain stable under the target reflection before it can supply a functional equation; matching the Euler product only in \(\operatorname{Re}s>1\) leaves the critical line and zero spectrum uncontrolled.

### 10. Seed

Can a prime-indexed transfer family be built on a state space where both reflected channels are jointly Hilbert–Schmidt or trace class near \(\operatorname{Re}s=1/2\), while preserving one copy of each commutative prime word and deriving the archimedean completion rather than appending it?

## Pass 26 — Theta-lattice control of a random Euler logarithm

### 1. Operator used

**COUNTEREXAMPLE-ANATOMY.** Locate the exact cancellation that makes the random Euler model nonvanishing to the right of one-half, then ask whether that cancellation is genuinely arithmetic or supplied only by random phases. The object is the random Euler product and its analytic logarithm, not a fitted finite matrix.

### 2. Generate three candidates

**26A — Theta-bounded random Euler logarithm.** For iid uniform unit-circle variables X_p, set Z_X(s) = product_p (1 − X_p p^(−s))^(−1) and L_X(s) = sum_p X_p p^(−s) + sum_{p,k≥2} X_p^k/(k p^(ks)). Claim: the exact integer-lattice theta identity bounds the variance of the multiplicative first-order term strongly enough to prove almost-sure locally uniform convergence of L_X, hence nonvanishing of Z_X, throughout Re(s)>1/2. **I-MULT:** unique factorization gives independent prime variables and the Euler-log expansion. **I-LATTICE:** Jacobi’s theta transformation θ(t)=t^(−1/2)θ(1/t) and its Mellin identity for the integer Dirichlet sum. **I-COUPLE:** the same variance sum generated by the prime factors is bounded by the full exact integer-lattice sum through sum_p p^(−2σ) ≤ sum_{n≥2} n^(−2σ).

**26B — Deterministic theta majorant for the prime log.** Define A_X(s) = sum_{p≤X} p^(−s) and the variance proxy V_X(σ) = sum_{p≤X} p^(−2σ), with the same theta-Mellin upper bound. Claim: the lattice bound can force cancellation or zero-freeness for the deterministic Euler product. **I-MULT:** the prime terms are the first Euler-log terms. **I-LATTICE:** the theta identity evaluates the integer-square majorant. **I-COUPLE:** the proposed transfer would have to turn the same lattice majorant into a deterministic bound for the unsquared prime sum.

**26C — Poisson-smoothed prime-power logarithm.** Let L_t(s) = sum_{p,k≥1} X_p^k p^(−ks) exp(−π k²(log p)²t)/k, and attempt to apply the Jacobi transform in t to obtain an exact s↔1−s relation while retaining the Euler logarithm as t↓0. **I-MULT:** the support and coefficients come from prime powers in the Euler logarithm. **I-LATTICE:** the proposed transform is Jacobi/Poisson summation on the integer lattice. **I-COUPLE:** it would require the prime-power-supported heat sum itself to transform into its dual while preserving the Euler coefficients.

### 3. Fast kill

- **26B:** Kill: the integer-lattice bound controls a second moment only after independent mean-zero phases are introduced; for deterministic X_p=1, the same step becomes an uncontrolled first-order sum and supplies no zero-free estimate.
- **26C:** Kill: Jacobi/Poisson duality applies to the complete lattice theta sum, while the prime-power mask is not a lattice; applying the transform to the completed lattice adds non-prime terms, and restricting back to prime powers destroys the exact transformation. It therefore has no proved coupling identity.
- **26A:** Keep for development: the variance bound and analytic logarithm give a concrete nonvanishing random model, and both named ingredients enter the same summability estimate.

### 4. Develop 26A: random nonvanishing from the coupled variance bound

Let X_p be independent and uniform on the unit circle. For a compact K inside Re(s)>1/2, choose a bounded open set U with K compactly contained in U and σ₀ = inf_{s∈U} Re(s)>1/2. In the Bergman Hilbert space A²(U), the partial sums S_N(s)=sum_{p≤p_N} X_p p^(−s) form a Hilbert-valued martingale. Independence and mean zero make distinct increments orthogonal, and

    sum_p E[||X_p p^(−s)||²_A²(U)]
      = integral_U sum_p p^(−2 Re(s)) dA(s)
      ≤ area(U) sum_p p^(−2σ₀)
      ≤ area(U) sum_{n≥2} n^(−2σ₀) < infinity.

The last comparison is exactly where multiplicative prime support meets the integer lattice. For u>1, define θ(t)=sum_{n∈Z} exp(−πn²t), with θ(t)=t^(−1/2)θ(1/t). Its Mellin identity is

    π^(−u/2) Γ(u/2) ζ(u)
      = (1/2) integral_0^infinity (θ(t)−1)t^(u/2−1) dt.

At u=2σ₀ this gives the exact finite majorant

    sum_p p^(−2σ₀) ≤ ζ(2σ₀)−1
      = π^σ₀/[2Γ(σ₀)] integral_0^infinity (θ(t)−1)t^(σ₀−1) dt − 1.

The theta transformation controls the small-t end; exponential decay controls the large-t end. Hilbert-valued martingale convergence gives almost-sure convergence in A²(U). The submean estimate for holomorphic functions upgrades this to uniform convergence on K. A countable exhaustion of Re(s)>1/2 makes the assertion simultaneous on the whole half-plane.

The higher Euler-log terms converge absolutely and locally uniformly: for Re(s)≥σ₀,

    sum_p sum_{k≥2} |X_p|^k/[k p^(k Re(s))]
      ≤ [1/(1−2^(−σ₀))] sum_p p^(−2σ₀)
      ≤ [ζ(2σ₀)−1]/[1−2^(−σ₀)] < infinity.

Thus L_X(s) converges locally uniformly almost surely. Each finite Euler product equals exp(L_{X,N}(s)); its locally uniform limit is exp(L_X(s)), which is holomorphic and never zero. This reproves the random-Euler theorem that the product is almost surely nonzero for every Re(s)>1/2. It does not assert nonvanishing for the deterministic zeta function.

**Explicit variance computation.** At σ=0.75, exact high-precision summation gives:

| prime cutoff X | V_X(0.75)=sum_{p≤X} p^(−3/2) | lattice upper bound for omitted primes 2/sqrt(X) |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 0.6894411241387444944952458455262585391543506172523957851 | 0.6324555320336758663997787088865437067439110278650433654 |
| 100 | 0.8176722524161450019929109595703938952580161883081674851 | 0.2000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000 |
| 1000 | 0.8422442834767334971471332192242641323830571049169130937 | 0.06324555320336758663997787088865437067439110278650433654 |
| 100000 | 0.8490867942369326745246603025079237977505540904157512802 | 0.006324555320336758663997787088865437067439110278650433654 |

The omitted-prime estimate follows from sum_{p>X}p^(−3/2) ≤ sum_{n>X}n^(−3/2) ≤ integral_X^infinity t^(−3/2)dt = 2/sqrt(X). The full variance majorant is ζ(3/2)−1 = 1.612375348685488343348567567924071630570800652400063408. These figures check the stated bound; they are not zero data and are not evidence about deterministic zeta zeros.

### 5. Source audit

The multiplicative input is unique factorization plus independence of X_p; the lattice input is the exact theta/Mellin identity and inclusion of the prime set in the integer lattice. Their interaction is the variance bound above. The positivity is an L² expectation, and the cancellation E[X_p]=0 is extra random input. Fixing all phases to 1 removes that cancellation, so the proof gives no deterministic sign or zero-free statement. Literature check: the same random-Euler nonvanishing theorem and Kolmogorov variance argument are already established in Lamzouri–Lester–Radziwiłł, *An effective universality theorem for the Riemann zeta function*, §2 (2018), https://doi.org/10.4171/CMH/448. The theta identity is an explicit repackaging of the summability bound, not a new mechanism.

### 6. Discrimination test

| world | result | exact step |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Curves over finite fields | **No Hasse reproduction** | Frobenius eigenvalues are deterministic geometric data; the independent mean-zero prime increments and their variance bound do not reconstruct the elliptic-curve degree pairing or prove the Hasse bound. |
| (b) Ihara zeta of Ramanujan graphs | **No Ramanujan bound** | Independent cycle phases yield a random product, but no nonbacktracking operator, graph adjacency spectrum, or Ramanujan bound is proved. |
| (c) Random Euler products with independent uniform phases | **TRUE-TOY RESULT REPRODUCED** | The Bergman martingale and theta-lattice variance bound prove almost-sure locally uniform convergence of the logarithm and nonvanishing of the product on Re(s)>1/2. |
| (d) Davenport–Heilbronn | **Fails I-MULT** | There is no canonical Euler factorization from which the independent X_p and prime-power logarithm are obtained; assigning them externally constructs a different random function. |
| (e) Beurling system with bad zeros | **Fails I-LATTICE / no deterministic discrimination** | The exact step sum_{q∈P_B}q^(−2σ) ≤ sum_{n≥2}n^(−2σ) is unavailable because generalized prime norms need not be ordinary integer lattice points, and there is no canonical ordinary theta transform for them. Even if a separate counting hypothesis made the randomized Beurling product converge, that random product would not constrain zeros of the deterministic Beurling zeta. |

### 7. Verdict

**PARTIAL — G0.** Candidate 26A passes the declared ingredient coupling and reproves the required random-Euler true-world result. It does not reach G1: the theorem is already standard, the theta insertion is a reformulation, and the proof’s mean-zero randomness has no deterministic counterpart. The d and e tests fail at the named multiplicative and lattice steps, respectively. The random-to-deterministic bridge remains open for a focused deepening pass.

### 8. New-idea check

The random Euler product’s almost-sure nonvanishing for Re(s)>1/2, its Euler-log decomposition, and Kolmogorov’s variance criterion are explicitly present in the literature cited above. The theta/Mellin expression for the integer Dirichlet sum is classical. The Hilbert-valued presentation is a standard analytic upgrade of pointwise martingale convergence, not a new theorem. The only project-specific observation is that the lattice sum can be named as the exact coupled majorant; it does not advance T.

### 9. Lesson

An exact lattice majorant can make a random multiplicative model converge, but the decisive cancellation is the independent phase mean zero. A viable deterministic candidate must replace that random cancellation by an arithmetic identity that also fails in Davenport–Heilbronn and bad-zero Beurling systems.

### 10. Seed

Can one extract a deterministic counterpart of E[X_p conjugate(X_q)]=1_{p=q} from a theta/Poisson identity that retains the single prime-power samples, and prove that the resulting identity fails for a Beurling system with bad zeros? **Next: DEEPEN(1 of 3), anchored on 26A.**

## Pass 27 — DEEPEN(1 of 3), deterministic Bohr flow versus random prime phases

### 1. Operator used

**IMPOSSIBILITY.** Deepen Pass 26A by replacing its independent phase variables with the deterministic vertical flow \(t\mapsto(p^{-it})_p\). Test whether long-time phase orthogonality can supply a deterministic version of the random variance argument, and locate the first exact obstruction if it cannot.

### 2. Generate three candidates

**27A — Bohr mean-square prime logarithm.** For a finite prime set \(I\) and \(\sigma>1/2\), define the Gram matrix \(K_T(p,q)=(pq)^{-\sigma}(2T)^{-1}\int_{-T}^T e^{-it\log(p/q)}dt\). Claim: unique factorization makes the vertical flow reproduce independent phase orthogonality in the long-time limit, and the theta-lattice bound on the resulting diagonal makes the deterministic prime logarithm converge analytically on \(\operatorname{Re}s>1/2\). **I-MULT:** unique factorization gives rational independence of the frequencies \(\log p\) and the Euler-log prime channels. **I-LATTICE:** Jacobi theta inversion and its Mellin identity evaluate the exact integer-square majorant. **I-COUPLE:** the same vertical mean that diagonalizes the multiplicative frequencies yields \(\sum_p p^{-2\sigma}\), which the integer lattice bounds.

**27B — Gaussian-window phase Gram.** Replace the sharp average over \([-T,T]\) by a centered Gaussian time law of variance \(T^2\), giving entries \((pq)^{-\sigma}\exp[-T^2\log^2(p/q)/2]\). Claim: theta control of the diagonal plus Gaussian suppression of all off-diagonal entries gives a uniform pointwise Euler-log bound, not merely a long-time mean.

**27C — Character-projected prime frequencies.** Average the prime Dirichlet polynomial over Dirichlet characters modulo a growing modulus and use the exact character projector to remove off-diagonal prime pairs. Claim: the character/lattice interaction leaves a positive, independently evaluated prime diagonal that can be upgraded to a deterministic analytic logarithm.

### 3. Fast kill

- **27B:** Kill the claimed pointwise upgrade: the Gaussian integral still computes an average in the external height parameter; a positive Gram and small off-diagonal entries control its averaged energy, not the value at each height. At height zero it retains the divergent deterministic sum.
- **27C:** Kill as an exact diagonalization: character orthogonality retains pairs in the same residue class, not only identical primes; this is the known character-orthogonality ratio-cross-term failure in the companion quadratic-search ledger. It supplies no exact one-index Euler logarithm or pointwise bound.
- **27A:** Develop the strongest candidate because it gives an exact finite identity, an integer-theta tail bound, and a clean test of whether time-averaged orthogonality upgrades to analytic convergence.

### 4. Develop 27A: exact Bohr orthogonality, mean convergence, and the pointwise obstruction

For a finite set of primes \(I\), put

\[
F_I(t)=\sum_{p\in I}p^{-\sigma-it},\qquad
K_T(p,q)=\frac1{2T}\int_{-T}^{T}p^{-\sigma-it}\overline{q^{-\sigma-it}}\,dt.
\]

Direct integration gives the exact formula

\[
K_T(p,q)=(pq)^{-\sigma}\,\operatorname{sinc}\!\left(T\log(p/q)\right),\qquad \operatorname{sinc}(x)=\begin{cases}\sin x/x,&x\ne0,\\1,&x=0.\end{cases}
\]

For every finite \(I\), \(K_T\) is positive semidefinite because it is the Gram matrix of the functions \(t\mapsto p^{-\sigma-it}\) in \(L^2([-T,T],dt/(2T))\). If \(p\ne q\), then \(\log(p/q)\ne0\), so \(K_T(p,q)\to0\); the diagonal is exactly \(p^{-2\sigma}\). Consequently

\[
\lim_{T\to\infty}\frac1{2T}\int_{-T}^{T}|F_I(t)|^2dt=\sum_{p\in I}p^{-2\sigma}.
\]

More generally, a monomial in finitely many phase coordinates has frequency \(\sum_p k_p\log p=\log(\prod_p p^{k_p})\). Unique factorization says this frequency is zero only when every integer \(k_p=0\). By the Kronecker–Weyl criterion, the finite vertical flow on the prime phase torus is equidistributed; its limiting polynomial moments agree with independent uniform unit phases. This is a deterministic *mean* realization of the finite-dimensional phase orthogonality, not a pointwise realization.

Define the Besicovitch mean-square seminorm by

\[
\|f\|_{B^2}^2=\limsup_{T\to\infty}\frac1{2T}\int_{-T}^{T}|f(t)|^2dt.
\]
For any finite prime interval \(M<p\le N\), the exact orthogonality identity gives

\[
\left\|\sum_{M<p\le N}p^{-\sigma-it}\right\|_{B^2}^2=\sum_{M<p\le N}p^{-2\sigma}.
\]
For \(\sigma>1/2\), these tails tend to zero. The exact integer-lattice majorant is

\[
\sum_p p^{-2\sigma}\le\sum_{n\ge2}n^{-2\sigma}=\zeta(2\sigma)-1<\infty.
\]
Writing \(\theta(u)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb Z}e^{-\pi n^2u}\), the Mellin identity

\[
\pi^{-v/2}\Gamma(v/2)\zeta(v)=\frac12\int_0^\infty(\theta(u)-1)u^{v/2-1}du,\qquad v=2\sigma>1,
\]
expresses that same diagonal-tail bound by the exact integer theta lattice. Thus the deterministic prime polynomials are Cauchy in \(B^2\) on every fixed line \(\operatorname{Re}s=\sigma>1/2\).

This is the precise limit of the method. For real \(s=\sigma\) with \(1/2<\sigma<1\), all terms are positive and

\[
\sum_p p^{-\sigma}\ge\sum_p p^{-1}=\infty.
\]
The divergence of \(\sum_p1/p\) is classical. Hence the deterministic prime-log partial sums diverge at the height \(t=0\), despite being Cauchy in the height-averaged \(B^2\) seminorm. A mean seminorm ignores individual zero-density sets of heights, including this exceptional point. It cannot yield locally uniform convergence of the analytic Euler logarithm on the half-plane. Exponentiating the mean-square object does not repair that missing pointwise analytic control or supply a zero-free theorem for the deterministic zeta function.

**Finite high-precision computation.** For \(\sigma=3/4\), \(I=\{2,3,5\}\), the entries of \(K_T\) are evaluated from the exact sinc formula. At 60-digit working precision:

```text

K=[[0.353553390593273762200422181052424519642418,
    0.253758647577951605153363235141039398364811,
    0.153968183590858935460000109342358600197422],
   [0.253758647577951605153363235141039398364811,
    0.192450089729875254836382926833985818549201,
    0.125567376829708707742278069769115065928342],
   [0.153968183590858935460000109342358600197422,
    0.125567376829708707742278069769115065928342,
    0.089442719099991587856366946749251049417625]]
eigenvalues=[0.000116752532301031014950242631003759803963155,
             0.0210598447194619808688663783410665352116011,
             0.614269602171377593009355433663591092593679]

T=10:   eigenvalues=[0.0841073307323028345265651738505610980664067,
                    0.182574523993293735040668553543341630931672,
                    0.368764344697544035325938327241758658611164]
T=100:  eigenvalues=[0.089404874667176677921302223476784961349045,

                    0.353553599726135449840745015770387943749278]
T→∞ diagonal=[0.353553390593273762200422181052424519642418,
```

All displayed finite matrices are PSD by their integral Gram representation. Their largest eigenvalue approaches \(2^{-3/2}<1\), but this is only the norm of a three-frequency mean-square Gram; it is not a contraction theorem for the Weil form and no conclusion follows from being below one.

### 5. Source audit

The finite Gram identity and its limit use only integration of exponentials. The full finite-torus equidistribution is Kronecker–Weyl, with rational independence supplied by unique factorization. The tail control uses the classical theta/Mellin identity. These ingredients interact exactly in a \(B^2\) norm, but that norm is an averaged-height object. The divergent real-axis prime sum is an unconditional counterexample to the hoped-for pointwise upgrade. No zero locations or target positivity are assumed. No analytic continuation of the \(B^2\) limit is proved.

### 6. Discrimination test

| world | result | exact step |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Curves over finite fields | **No Hasse reproduction** | The vertical prime-phase Gram has no finite-field Frobenius endomorphism or degree pairing, so it proves no Hasse bound. |
| (b) Ihara zeta of Ramanujan graphs | **No Ramanujan reproduction** | The construction contains no graph adjacency or nonbacktracking operator; phase orthogonality gives no Ramanujan eigenvalue bound. |
| (c) Random Euler products with independent uniform phases | **TRUE-TOY RESULT REPRODUCED** | For iid \(X_p\), the Bergman-space partial sums \(\sum_{p\le P}X_pp^{-s}\) are Hilbert-valued martingales on any bounded \(U\Subset\{\operatorname{Re}s>1/2\}\). Their increment second moments sum to at most \(|U|(\zeta(2\sigma_0)-1)\), \(\sigma_0=\inf_U\operatorname{Re}s>1/2\). Almost-sure \(A^2(U)\) convergence and the holomorphic submean inequality give local uniform convergence; the higher Euler-log powers converge absolutely, so exponentiation is holomorphic and nowhere zero. This is the same known G0 theorem, not a new result from the deterministic average. |
| (d) Davenport–Heilbronn | **Fails I-MULT** | The function supplies no canonical Euler prime coordinates or unique-factorization expansion from which these prime frequencies and logarithmic channels arise. Assigning primes externally changes the function being modeled. |
| (e) Beurling generalized primes with regular integer counts and bad zeros | **Fails I-LATTICE / fails discrimination** | The exact ordinary-integer theta transformation is absent for generalized prime norms. Moreover, any finite generalized-prime family with rationally independent log norms has the same Bohr mean Gram, so the positive mean-square orthogonality is not zero-selective; the method supplies no contradiction to the bad-zero model. |

### 7. Verdict

**PARTIAL — G0.** The pass proves an exact deterministic Besicovitch \(B^2\) Cauchy theorem for the prime logarithmic polynomial on each line \(\sigma>1/2\), and reproduces the established random-Euler nonvanishing theorem in the required toy world. It does not reach G1: the analytic convergence it would need is contradicted at \(t=0\) for every \(1/2<\sigma<1\). This is a scoped obstruction to upgrading this vertical-mean construction, not a no-go theorem for other deterministic arithmetic mechanisms.

### 8. New-idea check

Bohr/Kronecker–Weyl equidistribution, Besicovitch mean-square Dirichlet series, and the random Euler-product martingale argument are standard. The finite \(3\times3\) matrices are direct diagnostics. No novelty claim is made for the mean-square theorem. The candidate-specific result is the explicit identification of its exact boundary: the \(B^2\) tail is finite while the deterministic series diverges at the real height, so this coupling cannot furnish the desired locally uniform logarithm.

### 9. Lesson

Exact multiplicative orthogonality plus an integer-lattice variance bound produces convergence only after averaging over height; it does not control exceptional heights. A viable deterministic continuation must add a pointwise mechanism that survives the positive real axis, rather than infer local uniformity from a Besicovitch seminorm.

### 10. Seed

Can an independent deterministic arithmetic identity upgrade the vertical-flow \(B^2\) limit to locally uniform analytic convergence despite the divergence at \(t=0\), while still failing for bad-zero Beurling systems? **Next: DEEPEN(2 of 3), anchored on 26A.**


## Pass 28 — DEEPEN(2 of 3), prime-density subtraction and the floor-error remainder

### 1. Operator used

**INVARIANT.** Deepen 26A through the Pass 27 pointwise obstruction by subtracting the smooth prime-density integral and tracking the exact prime-counting discrepancy E(x)=π(x)−L₂(x), where L₂(x)=∫₂ˣdu/log u. Test if the exact integer floor identity controls this discrepancy independently of target-strength estimates.

### 2. Generate three candidates

**28A — Prime-density-subtracted Euler logarithm.** Define R_X(s)=Σ_{p≤X}p^(−s)−∫₂ˣu^(−s)/log(u)du. Claim: the divisor/floor identity and theta-lattice bound force locally uniform convergence for Re(s)>1/2. **I-MULT:** unique factorization and Λ(n)=Σ_{d|n}μ(d)log(n/d). **I-LATTICE:** exact Chebyshev floor identity Σ_{n≤x}log n=Σ_{d≤x}Λ(d)⌊x/d⌋. **I-COUPLE:** this identity is supposed to bound the E-weighted Stieltjes remainder of R_X.
 
**28B — Maximal inequality for deterministic vertical phases.** Use the Bohr Gram and theta majorant to upgrade Pass 27 mean-square convergence of Σ_{p≤X}p^(−σ−it) to pointwise convergence for each t. I-MULT is the Euler prime frequencies, I-LATTICE the theta/Mellin square-sum, and I-COUPLE a maximal bound turning their diagonal variance into pointwise control.

**28C — Möbius floor-error transform.** Set F(x)=Σ_{d≤x}Λ(d){x/d} and seek a Mellin transform that cancels composite channels while retaining a controlled prime term. I-MULT is von Mangoldt/Möbius inversion, I-LATTICE the exact floor identity, and I-COUPLE the claimed cancellation between F and the composite channels.

### 3. Fast kill

- **28B:** Kill: for fixed 1/2<σ<1, PNT partial summation gives Σ_{p≤X}p^(−σ−it) with main magnitude X^(1−σ)/(|1−s|log X); at t=0 its positive terms diverge. A maximal inequality for uncentered sums cannot prove convergence.
- **28C:** Kill as an independent evaluation: the exact identity leaves F(x), with only 0≤F(x)≤Σ_{d≤x}Λ(d)=O(x) from elementary bounds. It yields no square-root cancellation and reduces to 28A’s missing remainder estimate.
- **28A:** Develop: it gives an exact Stieltjes formula exposing the precise analytic quantity that a deterministic upgrade would need to bound.

### 4. Develop 28A

Stieltjes summation, with E right-continuous at prime jumps, gives exactly

R_X(s)=∫_{2⁻}ˣu^(−s)dE(u)
      =X^(−s)E(X)+s∫_{2⁻}ˣE(u)u^(−s−1)du.

If E(x)=O(x^θ), then both terms converge for Re(s)>θ. Therefore E(x)=Oε(x^(1/2+ε)) for every ε would suffice for convergence throughout Re(s)>1/2. That estimate is itself a target-strength prime-counting bound; the PNT estimate E=o(x/log x) does not make the integral absolutely convergent for 1/2<σ<1.

The exact floor identity also exposes the gap. For integer x, write F(x)=Σ_{d≤x}Λ(d){x/d}; then

xΣ_{d≤x}Λ(d)/d − F(x)
 = Σ_{n≤x}log n
 = x log x − x + O(log x).

The trivial F(x)≤Σ_{d≤x}Λ(d)=O(x) does not give the cancellation needed for E at a square-root scale. Theta inversion evaluates the complete integer sum, not this Λ-weighted fractional-part remainder.
#### Required random-toy reproduction

For iid uniform phases X_p and a bounded domain U compactly contained in Re(s)>1/2, set σ₀=inf_{s∈U}Re(s)>1/2. The partial sums Σ_{p≤P}X_pp^(−s) are Hilbert-valued martingales in A²(U), with orthogonal increments and total expected squared norm at most |U|Σ_p p^(−2σ₀)≤|U|(ζ(2σ₀)−1)<∞. Martingale convergence gives almost-sure convergence in A²(U); for each K compactly contained in U, the holomorphic submean inequality bounds the squared supremum of a tail on K by a constant times its A²(U) norm, yielding almost-sure uniform convergence on K. A countable exhaustion gives local uniform convergence in the half-plane. The k≥2 Euler-log terms converge absolutely and locally uniformly because Σ_pΣ_{k≥2}p^(−kσ)/k is bounded on each smaller half-plane by a constant times Σ_p p^(−2σ₀). Hence the full random logarithm converges locally uniformly and its exponential is holomorphic and nowhere zero. This supplies the required true-toy proof, but the decisive cancellation E[X_p]=0 is absent from the deterministic prime sum.

#### Explicit computation

A sieve and 50-digit evaluation of the logarithmic-integral term give these finite values. Stabilization is diagnostic only; no tail bound is established.

| 10,000 | −0.35956601340518201575617786789934561 | 0.37234990196583426622020084945681 + 0.12359655355301072430830395234784601 i |
| 100,000 | −0.37055409184430167795731814806453384 | 0.38043104340614965726117751697099333 + 0.11941748990434956092938220983302004 i |
| 1,000,000 | −0.37658130623515716042129662848810805 | 0.37634855873417770462501518012000080 + 0.11933378293579880781250880666852322 i |

The raw sums at s=3/4 are 6.8005324912718492969712202683081236, 9.7745131924025483017130142270766578, and 14.113979940778033269813842597642166, respectively.

### 5. Source audit

The exact coupling is Stieltjes summation plus the Chebyshev divisor identity. The hoped-for convergence comes only after a bound on E that these identities do not supply. The standard equivalence between π(x)=Li(x)+Oε(x^(1/2+ε)) for every ε>0 and the target zero-location statement makes that sufficient estimate circular as a proof route; see [AIM’s RH error-term reference](https://www.aimath.org/WWN/rh/articles/html/28a/). Prime-zeta Möbius inversion likewise shows the regularized prime sum carries the logarithm of ζ rather than an independent positivity mechanism.

### 6. Discrimination test

| world | result | exact step |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Curves over finite fields | **No Hasse reproduction** | The ordinary integer floor identity is not a finite-field degree pairing and yields no Frobenius bound. |
| (b) Ihara zeta of Ramanujan graphs | **No Ramanujan reproduction** | No nonbacktracking operator or graph trace identity is present. |
| (c) Random Euler products | **TRUE-TOY RESULT REPRODUCED** | Independent mean-zero phases make the prime-log increments orthogonal; Σ_p p^(−2σ)≤ζ(2σ)−1 and the Bergman-space martingale plus submean estimate give local uniform convergence and nonvanishing for Re(s)>1/2. This is the known Pass 26A argument, not a new deterministic estimate. |
| (d) Davenport–Heilbronn | **Fails I-MULT** | It has no canonical Euler factorization supplying the Λ divisor identity. |
| (e) Bad-zero Beurling systems | **Fails I-LATTICE** | Their generalized-prime weights do not satisfy the ordinary-integer floor identity; theta bounds the ordinary integer lattice, not their prime remainder. |

### 7. Verdict

**PARTIAL — G0.** The exact Stieltjes reduction is established and the random-Euler analogue reproduces the G0 toy theorem. The deterministic step reaches no new target result: its sufficient square-root-scale remainder estimate is already target-strength. The finite table has no tail certificate.

### 8. New-idea check

Prime-zeta Möbius inversion, PNT partial summation, the floor identity, and the square-root prime-counting error criterion are classical. The project-specific gain is a precise diagnosis of the Pass 27 gap: after subtracting the main term, the exact lattice identity leaves a Λ-weighted fractional-part remainder without an independent cancellation estimate. No new theorem is claimed.

### 9. Lesson

Subtracting the smooth density names the divergent remainder but does not create cancellation. Retain this route only if an exact theta/Poisson identity estimates F independently of target-strength prime-counting bounds.

### 10. Seed

**DEEPEN(3 of 3) of 26A:** test whether an exact theta/Poisson transform of F(x)=Σ_{d≤x}Λ(d){x/d} yields an independently bounded cancellation identity that fails in the bad-zero Beurling world. Require an explicit remainder estimate before proposing another locally uniform prime-log limit.

## Pass 29 — DEEPEN(3 of 3), CRT phase space and the identity-point obstruction

### 1. Operator used

**RISING-SEA.** Finish the Pass 26A deepening sequence by realizing phase variables on the profinite integer lattice rather than adjoining an abstract probability space. Test whether exact Chinese-remainder independence and theta variance control extend from Haar-almost-everywhere phase sequences to the identity point, where every Euler phase equals one.

### 2. Generate three candidates

**29A — Profinite CRT Euler model.** The profinite integers satisfy Z-hat ≅ product over primes p of Z_p. Use the base-p digits of each p-adic coordinate to produce a Haar-uniform phase X_p, and define Z_x(s)=product_p (1-X_p(x)p^(-s))^(-1). Claim: CRT independence plus the theta/Mellin bound proves local uniform convergence and nonvanishing almost surely, while prime-power sawtooth observables yield an exact arithmetic covariance identity. **I-MULT:** unique factorization gives the Euler factors and prime-power logarithm. **I-LATTICE:** CRT for integer residue lattices, p-adic digits, and the Jacobi theta/Mellin identity. **I-COUPLE:** the same p-adic coordinates produce local Euler phases and prime-power periodic channels; theta bounds the variance of those Euler channels.

**29B — Deterministic identity specialization.** Evaluate the same field at x=0 in Z-hat, where every digit is zero and every phase is one, and attempt to transfer the Haar convergence theorem to this arithmetic point. **I-MULT:** the Euler logarithm is the ordinary prime sum. **I-LATTICE:** zero is the identity residue in every finite CRT quotient. **I-COUPLE:** a claimed uniform evaluation inequality would transfer the theta-controlled mean-square bound to x=0.

**29C — Positive CRT reproducing kernel.** On G_P=product over primes p≤P of Z/pZ, search for a positive kernel whose normalized-Haar L2 norm controls evaluation at the identity uniformly in P while retaining the Euler-log variance estimate. **I-MULT:** coordinates correspond to distinct prime Euler factors. **I-LATTICE:** finite CRT and Fourier orthogonality on each cyclic residue group. **I-COUPLE:** the kernel must convert diagonal Euler variance into a uniform point-evaluation bound at the identity.

### 3. Fast kill

- **29B:** At the identity, X_p=1. For real 1/2<sigma<1, the prime sum sum_p p^(-sigma) diverges. Haar-almost-sure convergence therefore cannot imply convergence at this specific point.
- **29C:** On a finite group of size M with normalized counting measure, evaluation at one point has operator norm exactly sqrt(M), attained by its delta function. Since M tends to infinity, no uniform Haar-L2-to-point-evaluation bound exists in this norm.
- **29A:** Develop: it makes the random true-world model an explicit arithmetic-lattice probability space and quantifies the identity-point failure exactly.

### 4. Develop 29A: CRT-generated phases, exact prime-power covariance, and the missing point control

The Chinese remainder theorem gives the compact-group isomorphism Z-hat = inverse limit over m of Z/mZ ≅ product_p Z_p, with product Haar measure. Write x_p=sum_{j≥0} a_{p,j}p^j. Under Haar measure, the digits a_{p,j} are independent and uniform on {0,...,p-1}. Define U_p(x)=sum_{j≥0}a_{p,j}p^(-j-1) and X_p(x)=exp(2πi U_p(x)). The base-p expansion map sends Haar measure on Z_p to Lebesgue measure on [0,1], apart from the null set of nonunique expansions. Thus the X_p are independent uniform phases. At x=0, every digit is zero and X_p(0)=1.

For a compact K inside a bounded domain U whose closure lies in Re(s)>1/2, let sigma_0=inf_{s in U} Re(s)>1/2. The partial sums S_P(s,x)=sum_{p≤P} X_p(x)p^(-s) are Hilbert-valued martingales in the Bergman space A²(U): increments are independent, mean zero, and orthogonal, with

    sum_p E[||X_p p^(-s)||²_{A²(U)}] ≤ |U| sum_p p^(-2 sigma_0)
        ≤ |U|(zeta(2 sigma_0)-1) < infinity.

The last inequality is the integer-lattice majorant. Its theta representation follows from the Mellin identity

    pi^(-u/2) Gamma(u/2) zeta(u) = (1/2) integral_0^infinity (theta(t)-1)t^(u/2-1) dt,  u=2 sigma_0>1.

Hilbert-valued martingale convergence gives almost-sure convergence in A²(U). The holomorphic submean inequality upgrades this to uniform convergence on K; a countable compact exhaustion gives local uniform convergence on the half-plane Re(s)>1/2. For k≥2, the higher Euler-log terms converge absolutely and locally uniformly, bounded by a constant depending on sigma_0 times sum_p p^(-2 sigma_0). Therefore

    log Z_x(s)=sum_p sum_{k≥1} X_p(x)^k/(k p^(ks))

converges locally uniformly almost surely, and Z_x(s)=exp(log Z_x(s)) is holomorphic and nowhere zero. This proves the random Euler-product nonvanishing result on phases generated from the profinite integer lattice.

The integer lattice also carries exact centered sawtooth channels. For d≥2, define b_d(n)={n/d}-(d-1)/(2d), where {·} is fractional part. Its mean over residues modulo d is zero. If d=p^k divides e=p^ell, averaging over the e/d lifts of a residue r modulo d gives

    E[b_e(n) | n mod d=r] = b_d(r)/(e/d),
    E[b_d(n)b_e(n)] = (d²-1)/(12de).

If d=p^k and e=q^ell for distinct primes p and q, CRT independence and zero means give covariance zero. Let K_p=floor(log N/log p). Over one period L_N=lcm(1,...,N), the exact variance is

    Var_{n mod L_N}(sum_{p^k≤N} log(p)b_{p^k}(n))
      = sum_{p≤N} (log p)² sum_{k,ell≤K_p} (p^(2 min(k,ell))-1)/(12 p^(k+ell)).

This is a prime-separated positive covariance identity. It does not evaluate the deterministic prime-counting remainder from Pass 28; it is another exact representation of prime-supported data.

#### Explicit computations

The covariance formula gives exactly Cov(b_2,b_2)=1/16, Cov(b_2,b_4)=1/32, Cov(b_4,b_4)=5/64, and Cov(b_3,b_9)=2/81. Direct 60-digit summation of the prime-power variance gives:

| cutoff N | exact sawtooth variance |
|---:|---:|
| 10 | 0.9542278209707637625580265357525510353787235216570643202 |
| 100 | 27.43855696289905358981446265407982495752142654787597475 |
| 1,000 | 483.0971669755853078865562690532078474923385824743006513 |
| 10,000 | 6817.425367933064930904002435667779872381003398528939378 |

For G_P=product over p≤P of Z/pZ, let M_P=|G_P|=product over p≤P of p. With normalized counting measure, the norm of evaluation at one point is exactly sqrt(M_P). The finite values are:

| prime cutoff P | M_P | point-evaluation norm |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 5 | 30 | 5.4772255750516611345696978280080213 |
| 10 | 210 | 14.491376746189438573718664157169772 |
| 20 | 9,699,690 | 3114.4325325811763360527076054849581 |
| 30 | 6,469,693,230 | 80434.403273723613794183213192076737 |

The growth is exact: the evaluation norm is sqrt(product_{p≤P}p). Thus the Haar-L2 estimate cannot transfer to the identity through a uniform point-evaluation inequality. This does not exclude a different arithmetic estimate controlling the identity directly.

### 5. Source audit

The random positivity is an expectation of squared Bergman norms; its cancellation comes from Haar phases. Multiplicativity supplies the Euler-log expansion, while CRT supplies independent local coordinates and theta bounds the same variance series. This exact coupling controls an average over Z-hat, not the identity point. The random Euler nonvanishing theorem and its variance mechanism are established literature (Lamzouri–Lester–Radziwiłł, *An effective universality theorem for the Riemann zeta function*, 2018, https://doi.org/10.4171/CMH/448); the CRT realization and sawtooth covariance are elementary identities, not claimed as new literature results. No target positivity or zero location is assumed.

### 6. Discrimination test

| world | result | exact step |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Curves over finite fields | **No Hasse reproduction** | A profinite integer phase space has no Frobenius action or degree pairing and proves no elliptic-curve eigenvalue bound. |
| (b) Ihara zeta of Ramanujan graphs | **No Ramanujan reproduction** | The model contains no graph adjacency or nonbacktracking operator. |
| (c) Random Euler products with independent uniform phases | **TRUE-TOY RESULT REPRODUCED** | P-adic digit maps generate independent uniform phases; the Bergman martingale, theta variance bound, local uniform convergence, and exponentiation prove almost-sure nonvanishing for Re(s)>1/2. |
| (d) Davenport–Heilbronn | **Fails I-MULT** | There is no canonical Euler factorization assigning the prime channels. |
| (e) Bad-zero Beurling systems | **Fails I-LATTICE canonically** | There is no ordinary profinite integer ring whose CRT residues encode their generalized prime norms. If independent phases are attached externally, the same random theorem still holds and does not distinguish their bad-zero deterministic zeta function. |

### 7. Verdict

**PARTIAL — G0.** The construction realizes the random Euler theorem on the actual profinite integer lattice and proves an exact prime-power sawtooth covariance formula. The attempted deterministic transfer stops at a precise point: normalized-Haar L2 evaluation at the identity has norm sqrt(M_P), which diverges with the finite CRT quotient. This is an exact obstruction to mean-square-only transfer, not to every possible pointwise arithmetic mechanism.

### 8. New-idea check

The CRT product decomposition, p-adic digit maps, Hilbert-valued martingale convergence, and theta/Mellin majorant are standard. The covariance formula follows by a finite residue calculation. No new analytic number-theory theorem is claimed. The scoped diagnostic is the exact norm blowup of identity evaluation in the same finite residue models that generate the independent phases.

### 9. Lesson

The integer lattice can generate independent random Euler phases, but Haar averaging suppresses the one point that recovers the deterministic Euler product. Any next construction must control that point with an arithmetic estimate independent of the Haar-L2 norm and explain why the same control is unavailable in a bad-zero generalized-prime model.

### 10. Seed

**Pass 30 is consolidation.** Audit whether the new CRT identity-point obstruction is genuinely distinct from the Pass 27 exceptional-height obstruction; compare their exact seminorm/evaluation mechanisms, and decide whether a fresh pointwise arithmetic candidate is justified. Do not deepen 26A further: its three-pass allowance is exhausted.


## Pass 30 — Consolidation: averaged positivity and point evaluation

### 1. Verdicts and operator record

Passes 21–29 completed the scheduled nine-operator cycle, with Passes 26–29 deepening the random-Euler seed. No developed candidate reached G1. Passes 21–25 were DEAD; Passes 26–29 were PARTIAL at G0 because each reproduced a known toy result but did not supply the deterministic transfer. The RISING-SEA finite Brownian/gcd kernel check also crosses unit norm: lambda_max is 1.0109150546250736 on {2,3} and 1.5597157287383608 on {2,3,5}; only {2} is below one.

### 2. Coupling audit across Passes 21–29

The first five passes mostly failed because their finite reflection, Euler trace, or imported positivity did not distinguish the bad-zero model. The last four made multiplicativity and the integer lattice interact in a variance identity, but the resulting estimate controls an average rather than the deterministic evaluation needed at a specified point. Thus the search has begun to expose failures at the coupling/transfer step as well as at false-world discrimination, but it has not eliminated the false-world failure: the random-phase theorem itself remains true after phases are externally supplied to a bad-zero model.

Passes 27 and 29 exhibit one common functional-analytic obstruction. In the vertical-flow prime polynomial span, the B² seminorm is the weighted coefficient norm squared sum_p |c_p|² p^(-2 sigma), while evaluation at height zero is sum_p c_p p^(-sigma). On a finite prime set I, the norm of this evaluation functional is exactly sqrt(|I|), by Cauchy–Schwarz with equality when c_p is constant. It diverges as I grows. In the finite CRT group of size M with normalized counting measure, point evaluation has norm exactly sqrt(M), attained by the delta function. These are distinct concrete spaces and growth laws, but the same abstract issue: averaging norms do not yield uniform control at a selected point. Pass 29 strengthens the diagnosis with an arithmetic profinite realization; it does not establish a new general no-go beyond point evaluation in these specified norms.

### 3. Scoped no-go result

**Proposition (no uniform point-evaluation bound in normalized finite CRT L²).** Let G be a finite group of cardinality M, with normalized counting measure, and let E_x(f)=f(x) for a fixed x in G. Then the operator norm of E_x on L²(G) is sqrt(M). Consequently, for any sequence of finite CRT groups whose cardinalities tend to infinity, no constant C independent of the quotient can satisfy |f(x)|≤C||f||₂ for all functions on every quotient.

**Proof.** The delta function 1_{x} has normalized L² norm M^(-1/2) and E_x(1_x)=1, giving ||E_x||≥sqrt(M). Conversely, the normalized norm satisfies ||f||₂²=M^(-1)sum_{y∈G}|f(y)|², so |f(x)|²≤sum_y|f(y)|²=M||f||₂². Hence ||E_x||≤sqrt(M), proving equality. The result applies to normalized finite quotient L² only; it does not rule out a different norm, a restricted function class, or a direct arithmetic pointwise estimate.

This proposition is an exact scoped theorem already used in Pass 29C, not a new general analytic-number-theory result. The comparison with Pass 27 is a classification: both are instances of unbounded evaluation relative to an averaged norm, although the index sets and seminorms differ.

### 4. Selection-bias and seed audit

The older passes over-selected positive objects whose sign was generic under substitution of generalized primes. The newer random/Bohr passes improve the coupling by using unique factorization and theta summability in the same variance estimate, but the final upgrade still fails at pointwise evaluation. Do not infer a pointwise limit from almost-sure convergence, a Bohr mean, or a finite Gram norm without a uniform evaluation estimate.

Pass 26A and 27A have exhausted their three-deepening allowance. Pass 28A has no independent target-scale remainder estimate and should not be deepened without a new exact identity. Select 29A for one further pass: it reached G0 in the random-phase toy world, and its next test should seek a deterministic identity-point estimate derived directly from arithmetic data, then apply the bad-zero Beurling test before developing any positivity claim.

### 5. Next operator schedule

Pass 31 begins a new rotation at TOY-FIRST. Use DEEPEN(1 of 3) on seed 29A only if the candidate changes the pointwise control mechanism; an equivalent Haar or Bohr average is a fast kill. Generate three concrete candidates, apply I-MULT/I-LATTICE/I-COUPLE before development, and keep the distinction between an exact finite evaluation bound and a limiting arithmetic estimate explicit.

### 6. Consolidation verdict

The exact finite CRT evaluation norm is proved, and it corroborates the vertical-flow exceptional-height obstruction as an instance of the same averaged-norm versus point-evaluation gap. There is no G1 result and no broader no-go theorem. Pass 31 should test a genuinely pointwise arithmetic estimate, not another change of averaging space.

## Pass 31 — DEEPEN(1 of 3) under TOY-FIRST: CRT Sobolev pinning

### 1. Operator used

**TOY-FIRST.** Continue seed 29A by comparing the random-phase true toy model with a finite profinite CRT model, but change the control mechanism: impose a positive tensor Sobolev norm that makes evaluation at the identity bounded, and calculate whether the Euler prime modes remain bounded in that same norm.

### 2. Generate three candidates

**31A — Tensor CRT Sobolev pinning.** On G_P=product_{p≤P} Z/pZ with normalized Haar measure, give a tensor Fourier character indexed by k=(k_p) squared weight product_{p≤P}(1+p^alpha lambda_p(k_p)), where lambda_p(k)=4 sin²(pi k/p). Claim: for alpha large enough, identity evaluation is uniformly bounded as P grows, while the same norm may still accommodate the prime Euler polynomial. **I-MULT:** one coordinate and one character encode each prime Euler factor. **I-LATTICE:** finite CRT Fourier analysis and the integer theta/Mellin variance majorant. **I-COUPLE:** the same prime character has coefficient p^(-s) in the Euler polynomial and eigenvalue lambda_p(1) in the point-pinning energy.

**31B — All-zero-cylinder martingale bound.** For the p-adic digit martingale U_{p,J}=sum_{j<J}a_{p,j}p^(-j-1), condition on the all-zero length-J cylinder and compare its measure p^(-J) with the conditional Euler-log mean. Claim: theta-controlled unconditional variance plus the exact cylinder probability yields a pointwise bound at x=0 independent of J. **I-MULT:** the conditioned prime increments remain the Euler-log channels. **I-LATTICE:** exact p-adic cylinder measures and theta summability. **I-COUPLE:** compare the deterministic all-zero conditional value with its variance using the same cylinder filtration.

**31C — Fourier delta pinning with arithmetic weights.** Use the exact finite CRT identity delta_0(x)=M^(-1)sum_chi chi(x), then weight its characters by the prime Euler coefficients and the theta majorant. Claim: a signed arithmetic Fourier pinning kernel can retain the identity value while its dual norm stays uniformly bounded. **I-MULT:** prime Euler coefficients label the local characters. **I-LATTICE:** CRT Fourier inversion and theta/Mellin. **I-COUPLE:** the same character sum must both reconstruct x=0 and bound the Euler prime sum.

### 3. Fast kill

- **31B:** The conditional expectation bound at the all-zero cylinder loses the inverse cylinder probability. Cauchy–Schwarz gives a factor p^(J/2) for the local evaluation; multiplying independent coordinates cannot make this uniform without additional regularity. This is the same point-evaluation loss, not a new mechanism.
- **31C:** Exact Fourier inversion reconstructs delta_0, whose normalized Haar L2 norm is sqrt(M). Reweighting its Fourier coefficients changes the represented function or incurs the reciprocal dual norm; no claimed uniform bound follows from theta control alone.
- **31A:** Develop the strongest survivor: the tensor norm explicitly trades point-evaluation continuity against the size of each prime mode, so both sides can be compared exactly.

### 4. Develop 31A: exact evaluation/prime-energy tradeoff

For one prime p, the characters chi_{p,k}(x)=exp(2 pi i kx/p), k=0,...,p-1, diagonalize the cycle first-difference Laplacian, with eigenvalues lambda_p(k)=4 sin²(pi k/p). Give the tensor character chi_k on G_P squared norm weight W_P(k)=product_{p≤P}(1+w_p lambda_p(k_p)), where w_p=p^alpha and alpha>2. The squared operator norm of evaluation at the identity is the reciprocal-weight sum

    E_P² = sum_{k in product Z/pZ} 1/W_P(k)
         = product_{p≤P} A_p,
    A_p = sum_{k=0}^{p-1} 1/(1+p^alpha lambda_p(k)).

This factorization is exact by the product structure. For j=min(k,p-k), sin(pi j/p)≥2j/p, hence lambda_p(k)≥16j²/p². Therefore

    A_p-1 ≤ (pi²/48) p^(2-alpha).

Since sum_p p^(-r) converges for r>1, alpha>3 gives a finite product bound independent of P. Conversely, the k=1 term gives A_p-1≥(1+4 pi² p^(alpha-2))^(-1). For alpha≤2 this stays bounded below; for 2<alpha≤3 it is bounded below by a positive constant times p^(2-alpha), whose prime sum diverges. Thus, within this power-weight family, uniform boundedness of the evaluation product holds exactly when alpha>3; this tensor regularity fixes the finite-quotient evaluation blowup.

Now let F_P(s,x)=sum_{p≤P}p^(-s)chi_{p,1}(x_p), the simplest finite random-character Euler channel. Its squared tensor Sobolev norm is exactly

    ||F_P(s)||_(H_P)² = sum_{p≤P} p^(-2 sigma)(1+p^alpha lambda_p(1)),  sigma=Re(s).

Using sin(pi/p)≥2/p gives lambda_p(1)≥16/p², so the summand is at least 16 p^(alpha-2-2sigma). For 1/2<sigma<1, boundedness of these norms would require alpha<2sigma+1<3. But uniform identity evaluation in this power-weight tensor family requires alpha>3. The requirements have empty intersection. This is an exact obstruction for the declared family of first-difference tensor weights; it is not a no-go for non-product norms or direct arithmetic pointwise estimates.

For a concrete numerical check, use alpha=5 and sigma=3/4. At each p the local evaluation factor A_p and the partial product are computed at 60-digit precision. The prime-character energy contribution is p^(-3/2)(1+p^5 lambda_p(1)).
