### 5. Source audit

The positive norm is a designed tensor Sobolev norm, and its evaluation bound follows from finite Fourier duality. The theta identity only supplies the ordinary Haar variance bound sum_p p^(-2sigma); it does not pay the added local Sobolev energy. The exponent conflict alpha>3 versus alpha<2sigma+1 is proved for 1/2<sigma<1 and the specified first-difference product weights. No zero location or target inequality is assumed. This does not prove that every pointwise arithmetic mechanism has the same cost.

### 6. Discrimination test

| world | result | exact step |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Curves over finite fields | **No Hasse reproduction** | The CRT Sobolev group has no Frobenius action or degree pairing; its evaluation inequality gives no bound on curve-zeta eigenvalues. |
| (b) Ihara zeta of Ramanujan graphs | **No Ramanujan reproduction** | The finite product of cyclic prime-coordinate groups has no graph adjacency or nonbacktracking operator. |
| (c) Random Euler products with independent uniform phases | **The unchanged G0 theorem is reproduced; the pinning upgrade fails** | The Bergman martingale and theta variance bound prove almost-sure local uniform convergence and nonvanishing for the random Euler product on Re(s)>1/2. The new Sobolev norm cannot stay bounded on the prime series in 1/2<sigma<1, so it adds no pointwise deterministic control. |
| (d) Davenport–Heilbronn | **Fails I-MULT** | There is no canonical prime Euler factorization assigning the characters; externally attaching the coordinates changes the modeled function. |
| (e) Bad-zero Beurling systems | **Fails discrimination** | The finite Sobolev evaluation calculation depends only on a labeled product of cyclic groups and positive weights; it survives for arbitrary labeled prime channels. If the ordinary CRT lattice is removed, I-LATTICE has no canonical instantiation, and externally supplied phases still reproduce only the random theorem. |

### 7. Verdict

**DEAD for the deterministic bridge; G0 for the inherited random toy theorem.** Tensor Sobolev pinning makes evaluation uniformly bounded only by choosing alpha>3, while the prime Euler modes have unbounded norm throughout 1/2<sigma<1 under that same choice. The new estimate is exact for this product norm family and cannot be promoted to a pointwise Euler-log bound.

### 8. New-idea check

The finite Fourier evaluation formula, cycle-Laplacian eigenvalues, and tensor-product Hilbert norm are standard. The project-specific result is the explicit exponent incompatibility for this chosen way of adding p-adic point regularity to the random Euler channels. It is a scoped calculation, not a universal obstruction theorem; no novelty claim beyond this candidate-specific diagnostic is made.

### 9. Lesson

Adding enough product Sobolev regularity can tame identity evaluation, but its local weights overcharge the prime characters: for 1/2<sigma<1, the embedding and Euler-energy exponent ranges do not overlap. A next attempt must obtain pointwise control from a norm or arithmetic identity whose regularity cost is not a product of independent local first differences.

### 10. Seed

Can a non-product, multiplicatively coupled norm use the exact integer theta transform to control the profinite identity while charging only the diagonal variance sum, and can its analogue fail in a bad-zero Beurling system? **Next: DEEPEN(2 of 3) of 29A, unless the candidate repeats this same product-weight tradeoff.**

## Pass 32 — DEEPEN(2 of 3) under SPECIALIZE: identity evaluation versus prime energy

### 1. Operator used

**SPECIALIZE.** Fix one real parameter (1/2<\sigma<1), and isolate the deterministic identity-point value of the finite prime Euler logarithm. Test whether an arbitrary Hilbert geometry—not merely the tensor first-difference norm of Pass 31—can make point evaluation uniformly bounded while keeping the prime polynomial bounded in norm. The same prime modes are also tested under independent Haar phases, where the theta/Mellin lattice estimate gives a true random-Euler theorem.

### 2. Generate three candidates

**32A - General Hilbert identity embedding.** Let \(G_X=\mathbb T^{\{p\le X\}}\) have independent Haar coordinates \(z_p\), and define
\[
P_X(\sigma,z)=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}z_p.
\]
For each \(X\), equip the span of these prime characters with any positive-definite Hilbert norm \(H_X\), allowing arbitrary non-diagonal cross terms. Claim: uniform boundedness of evaluation \(E_X(f)=f(1,1,\ldots)\) and boundedness of \(\|P_X\|_{H_X}\) are incompatible. **I-MULT:** unique factorization assigns one coordinate and coefficient \(p^{-s}\) to each Euler factor. **I-LATTICE:** for \(x_p=\sum_{j\ge0}a_{p,j}p^j\in\mathbb Z_p\), use the measurable digit map \(U_p=\sum_{j\ge0}a_{p,j}p^{-j-1}\pmod1\) and \(z_p=e^{2\pi iU_p}\). Haar digits are independent uniform base-\(p\) digits, so \(U_p\) is uniform on \([0,1]\); truncating at \(J\) uses the exact finite CRT quotient \(G_{P,J}=\prod_{p\le P}\mathbb Z/p^J\mathbb Z\) and its \(p^J\)-point uniform grid. The Jacobi theta Mellin identity
\[
\int_0^\infty(\theta(t)-1)t^{\sigma-1}dt=2\Gamma(\sigma)\pi^{-\sigma}\zeta(2\sigma),\qquad \sigma>1/2,
\]
gives \(\sum_p p^{-2\sigma}\le\zeta(2\sigma)-1\). **I-COUPLE:** the same CRT character is mean-zero under Haar measure but equals 1 at the identity, so its prime sum has bounded mean-square energy and an unbounded identity value.

**32B — Identity-centered Euler logarithm.** Replace each mode by \(p^{-\sigma}(z_p-1)\) so that the finite sum vanishes at the identity, and use its Haar norm as the positive energy. Claim: this centered object retains the original Euler-log information while repairing the evaluation loss. **I-MULT:** the subtraction is performed factor-by-factor in the logarithm of the Euler product. **I-LATTICE:** the constant mode is the zero CRT character and is separated by exact finite Fourier orthogonality; theta controls the nonconstant variance. **I-COUPLE:** test whether removing the identity value also removes only a harmless lattice constant, rather than the divergent deterministic prime sum.

**32C — Abel-damped identity embedding.** Set \(P_{X,\varepsilon}(\sigma)=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-(\sigma+\varepsilon)}\) and let \(\varepsilon\downarrow0\) after the prime cutoff. Claim: Abel damping gives a cutoff-uniform identity value while retaining the same limiting Euler logarithm. **I-MULT:** the damping shifts the exponent in each prime Euler factor. **I-LATTICE:** use the CRT identity and the theta Mellin majorant for the squared coefficients. **I-COUPLE:** the same damping must make the first-power prime sum converge at the identity without losing the square-summable random variance.

### 3. Fast kill

- **32B:** At the identity the centering subtracts \(S_X=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}\). Restoring the original Euler logarithm restores this scalar; without it the candidate is a different, normalized product. Moreover, \(\|\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}(z_p-1)\|_2^2=S_X^2+\sum_{p\le X}p^{-2\sigma}\), so the centered polynomial is not bounded in the positive Haar norm.
- **32C:** For \(\sigma+\varepsilon\le1\), the identity sum still diverges as \(X\to\infty\). Choosing \(\varepsilon>1-\sigma\) makes each sum finite, but the values increase without bound as \(\varepsilon\downarrow0\); no uniform identity bound survives the claimed limit.
- **32A:** Develop. It is the strongest survivor as an exact obstruction theorem for all positive Hilbert norms on the declared finite prime spans, and it directly subsumes the specific product-weight conflict from Pass 31.

### 4. Develop 32A: an arbitrary-metric evaluation/energy theorem

For \(X\ge2\), let
\[
P_X=\{p:p\le X\},\qquad
F_X(z)=\sum_{p\in P_X}p^{-\sigma}z_p,
\qquad
S_X=\sum_{p\in P_X}p^{-\sigma}.
\]
Let \(V_X=\operatorname{span}\{z_p:p\in P_X\}\), and let \(H_X\) be any Hilbert norm on \(V_X\). No diagonal, tensor, locality, or product assumption is made. Write
\[
C_X=\|E_X\|_{H_X^*},\qquad E_X(f)=f(1,1,\ldots).
\]
The exact identity is
\[
E_X(F_X)=S_X.
\]
Therefore Hilbert duality gives the finite, quantitative inequality
\[
\boxed{\quad \|F_X\|_{H_X}\ge {S_X\over C_X}.\quad} \tag{32.1}
\]
This immediately rules out the simultaneous bounds \(\sup_X C_X<\infty\) and \(\sup_X\|F_X\|_{H_X}<\infty\), once \(S_X\to\infty\). The conclusion is independent of all off-diagonal entries of the Gram matrix.

Here is an elementary proof of the required divergence, without the prime number theorem. Since \(p^{-\sigma}\ge p^{-1}\) for \(\sigma<1\),
\[
S_X\ge\sum_{p\le X}{1\over p}.
\]
Every integer \(n\le X\) occurs in the Euler expansion of \(\prod_{p\le X}(1-1/p)^{-1}\). Hence
\[
\log H_{\lfloor X\rfloor}
\le\sum_{p\le X}-\log(1-1/p)
\le\sum_{p\le X}{1\over p}
       +2\sum_{p\le X}{1\over p^2}.
\]
Using \(\sum_{p}p^{-2}\le\sum_{n\ge2}n^{-2}=\pi^2/6-1\), it follows that
\[
S_X\ge \log\log X-2(\pi^2/6-1)\longrightarrow\infty. \tag{32.2}
\]
Thus (32.1) is a genuine no-go for uniformly bounded point evaluation plus uniformly bounded prime-polynomial energy, including every non-diagonal Hilbert metric. It does not exclude direct arithmetic pointwise estimates, renormalizations that retain and separately control the removed scalar, or methods that do not impose a uniform Hilbert evaluation bound.

#### Exact comparison with the random-phase toy

Under product Haar measure on \(\mathbb T^{P_X}\), independence and \(\mathbb E z_p=0\) give
\[
\mathbb E|F_X|^2=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-2\sigma}
\le\zeta(2\sigma)-1.
\]
The bound is uniform in \(X\); explicitly, with \(\theta(t)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb Z}e^{-\pi n^2t}\), \(\int_0^\infty(\theta(t)-1)t^{\sigma-1}dt=2\Gamma(\sigma)\pi^{-\sigma}\zeta(2\sigma)\) for \(\sigma>1/2\). At the identity, however, \(F_X(1)=S_X\to\infty\). Thus the finite Haar energy and the deterministic identity value are not contradictory: the identity is a measure-zero evaluation, and the norm of evaluation cannot be uniform.

The associated random Euler product also has a complete toy proof. Let \(Z_p\) be independent uniform unit-circle variables and put
\[
\mathcal Z(s)=\prod_p(1-Z_pp^{-s})^{-1}.
\]
On any bounded domain \(D\) whose closure lies in \(\Re s>1/2\), the partial sums \(\sum_{p\le X}Z_pp^{-s}\) form a Hilbert-valued martingale in the Bergman space \(A^2(D)\). Its total increment variance is
\[
\sum_p\mathbb E\|Z_pp^{-s}\|_{A^2(D)}^2
=\int_D\sum_p p^{-2\Re s}\,dA(s)
\le |D|\bigl(\zeta(2\sigma_0)-1\bigr)<\infty,
\]
where \(\sigma_0=\inf_{s\in D}\Re s>1/2\). The Hilbert martingale converges almost surely in \(A^2(D)\). Bergman point-evaluation estimates on compact subsets then give almost-sure locally uniform convergence of the analytic prime series. The remaining logarithmic Euler terms converge absolutely and locally uniformly because
\[
\sum_p\sum_{k\ge2}{p^{-k\sigma_0}\over k}
\le\sum_p{p^{-2\sigma_0}\over1-p^{-\sigma_0}}<\infty.
\]
Consequently \(\mathcal Z(s)=\exp(\sum_p\sum_{k\ge1}Z_p^kp^{-ks}/k)\) almost surely on the half-plane, and is nowhere zero there. This proves the true random-Euler analogue. It does not provide uniform evaluation at the all-ones identity: the random theorem holds almost surely, while the identity-point prime sum diverges.

#### High-precision finite profile

At \(\sigma=3/4\), the following sums were evaluated with 60-digit arithmetic. The last column is \(S_X/(\mathbb E|F_X|^2)^{1/2}\), a lower bound for the evaluation norm of any Hilbert geometry that leaves the prime polynomial at no greater norm than its Haar \(L^2\) norm.

### 5. Source audit

The random positive energy comes from Haar orthogonality and the integer-lattice theta/Mellin variance bound. The obstruction comes from a different, exact coupling: multiplicativity assigns the prime coefficients, CRT makes every prime character equal to 1 at the identity, and Hilbert duality forces the norm to pay for their divergent sum. No target inequality or zero information is used. The argument is an elementary functional-analytic lemma specialized to prime Euler sums; it is not a new theorem about zeta zeros. The random Euler model and its almost-sure analytic nonvanishing are also treated in Lamzouri, Lester, and Radziwiłł, “An effective universality theorem for the Riemann zeta function” (2018), https://arxiv.org/abs/1611.10325.

### 6. Discrimination test

| world | result | exact step |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Curves over finite fields | No Hasse analogue | The construction has no curve, Frobenius action, or degree pairing; its point-evaluation inequality gives no eigenvalue bound. |
| (b) Ihara zeta of Ramanujan graphs | No Ramanujan analogue | There is no graph adjacency or nonbacktracking operator. |
| (c) Random Euler products with independent uniform phases | TRUE-TOY RESULT REPRODUCED | The Bergman martingale, theta/Mellin variance estimate, absolute higher-power remainder, and exponential representation prove almost-sure local uniform convergence and nonvanishing for \(\Re s>1/2\). |
| (d) Davenport–Heilbronn | Fails I-MULT | It has no canonical Euler factors to define the prime-coordinate random series; arbitrary Dirichlet coefficients do not instantiate this construction. |
| (e) Beurling generalized primes with bad prime counts | Fails canonical I-LATTICE; no discrimination if phases are attached externally | The Jacobi theta transform and ordinary CRT identification are integer-lattice inputs, not canonical Beurling data. If independent phases are attached by hand, the same random nonvanishing proof goes through and does not distinguish the deterministic bad-zero system. |

### 7. Verdict

**PARTIAL — G0.** The random true toy is reproved, and the deterministic bridge is ruled out for every family of Hilbert norms that simultaneously has uniformly bounded identity evaluation and uniformly bounded norm of the uncentered prime polynomial. The theorem subsumes the product-weight obstruction of Pass 31. It does not address direct pointwise arithmetic estimates or a different object that avoids this Hilbert embedding.

### 8. New-idea check

The core inequality is the standard dual-norm estimate \(|E(f)|\le\|E\|\|f\|\); divergence of \(\sum_p1/p\) is classical; Hilbert-valued martingale convergence and Bergman evaluation are standard. The new project-level contribution is the explicit arbitrary-metric formulation tying these facts to the exact CRT identity value, which broadens Pass 31 beyond diagonal or tensor weights. No novelty claim is made for the underlying theorem or for the random Euler result.

### 9. Lesson

Changing the diagonal weights was not the essential issue: any Hilbert norm that both uniformly controls the identity and keeps the full prime polynomial bounded contradicts its exact identity value. A surviving pointwise mechanism must therefore avoid at least one of those two requirements—e.g. control the identity directly by an arithmetic estimate, or separate a renormalized scalar while proving an independent bound for it—and must still fail canonically on bad-zero Beurling data.

### 10. Seed

**29A remains eligible; next is DEEPEN(3 of 3).** Test a concrete arithmetic pointwise estimate that is not obtained from a uniformly bounded Hilbert evaluation functional. It must retain the removed first-power prime scalar, use an explicit ordinary-lattice identity in the same estimate, and demonstrate why the corresponding estimate cannot be copied to a bad-zero Beurling system. Reject another average, diagonal weight, centered prime polynomial, or Abel limit before development.

## Pass 33 - DEEPEN(3 of 3) under GENERALIZE: rare CRT cylinders and conditional prime concentration

### 1. Operator used

**GENERALIZE.** Replace the single identity evaluation from Pass 32 by a whole finite neighborhood in the profinite integer lattice. For each prime cutoff \(X\) and digit depth \(J\), condition the independent p-adic phase coordinates to their first \(J\) zero digits; compute the conditional prime-sum mean, variance, and exact cylinder mass. The question is whether this direct pointwise-neighborhood estimate can retain the first-power Euler scalar without assuming a uniform Hilbert evaluation bound.

### 2. Generate three candidates

**33A - Identity-cylinder concentration.** Let \(U_p\) be the base-\(p\) digit image of Haar measure on \(\mathbb Z_p\), let \(z_p=e^{2\pi iU_p}\), and define \(F_X(\sigma)=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}z_p\). Condition on \(C_{X,J}=\{U_p\in[0,p^{-J})\text{ for every }p\le X\}\). Claim: on this exact CRT cylinder, the conditional real mean grows like a positive multiple of \(\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}\), while the conditional variance remains bounded and the cylinder mass is exactly \(e^{-J\theta(X)}\). **I-MULT:** unique factorization labels one independent local phase and one \(p^{-\sigma}\) coefficient per Euler prime. **I-LATTICE:** p-adic digits and finite CRT quotients define the cylinders, while the Jacobi theta Mellin identity bounds \(\sum_p p^{-2\sigma}\). **I-COUPLE:** the same local prime coordinate determines both the conditional phase mean and its exact cylinder probability, so the arithmetic prime sum and the lattice rarity appear in one conditional concentration inequality.

**33B - Möbius-inverted prime-zeta continuation.** Use \(P(s)=\sum_p p^{-s}=\sum_{k\ge1}\mu(k)\log\zeta(ks)/k\) initially for \(\Re s>1\), and use the theta functional equation to continue the zeta factors. Claim: the signed inversion and lattice functional equation give a pointwise prime-log identity on \(1/2<\Re s<1\) without a zero-free assumption. **I-MULT:** Möbius inversion of the Euler-log identity extracts the prime term. **I-LATTICE:** the Jacobi theta transformation continues the completed zeta factors. **I-COUPLE:** the same Möbius sum must remain single-valued and controlled when its \(k=1\) term crosses the critical strip.

**33C - Positive CRT Fejér pinning.** Convolve \(F_X\) with the product of one-dimensional Fejér kernels on the finite CRT phase grids, then evaluate at the identity. Claim: a positive approximate identity smooths the prime sum enough to give a cutoff-uniform value without removing its Euler coefficients. **I-MULT:** the \(p\)-coordinate Fourier mode carries coefficient \(p^{-s}\). **I-LATTICE:** exact finite CRT Fourier inversion and the theta Mellin variance bound control the positive kernel. **I-COUPLE:** the multiplier of each prime character under the same positive kernel must both preserve the Euler term and regularize its identity sum.

### 3. Fast kill

- **33B:** Möbius inversion is exact only in its initial absolutely convergent region. The continued expression contains \(\log\zeta(s)\) at \(k=1\); defining a single analytic logarithm across the target strip requires excluding zeros there, while local branches do not provide the claimed global pointwise estimate. This is circular for the proposed continuation.
- **33C:** The Fejér multiplier at the prime character \(z_p\) is \(N_p/(N_p+1)\). For every nontrivial Fejér kernel \(N_p\ge1\), this is at least \(1/2\), so the smoothed identity value is at least \(\frac12\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}\) and still diverges. Setting \(N_p=0\) deletes that prime mode rather than approximating the identity.
- **33A:** Develop. Unlike the previous Hilbert-norm argument, it gives an exact conditional probability and concentration estimate on finite neighborhoods of the identity.

### 4. Develop 33A: exact concentration on a rare arithmetic cylinder

Let \(1/2<\sigma<1\), \(J\ge1\), and \(X\ge3\). In the profinite integer model write
\[
x_p=\sum_{j\ge0}a_{p,j}p^j\in\mathbb Z_p,\qquad
U_p=\sum_{j\ge0}a_{p,j}p^{-j-1}\in[0,1],
\qquad z_p=e^{2\pi iU_p}.
\]
Under Haar measure, the digits \(a_{p,j}\) are independent uniform elements of \(\{0,\ldots,p-1\}\), so \(U_p\) is uniform on \([0,1]\), independently for distinct primes. The event that its first \(J\) digits vanish is exactly \(U_p\in[0,p^{-J})\), with probability \(p^{-J}\). Thus the simultaneous finite-CRT cylinder
\[
C_{X,J}=\bigcap_{p\le X}\{U_p\in[0,p^{-J})\}
\]
has exact mass
\[
\mathbb P(C_{X,J})=\prod_{p\le X}p^{-J}
=\exp\!\left(-J\sum_{p\le X}\log p\right)=e^{-J\theta(X)}. \tag{33.1}
\]

For a single prime coordinate, conditional integration over this interval gives
\[
\mu_{p,J}:=\mathbb E[z_p\mid U_p<p^{-J}]
=p^J\int_0^{p^{-J}}e^{2\pi iu}\,du
=e^{\pi i/p^J}\frac{\sin(\pi/p^J)}{\pi/p^J}.
\]
In particular,
\[
\Re\mu_{p,J}=\frac{\sin(2\pi/p^J)}{2\pi/p^J}.
\]
For every \(p\ge3\) and \(J\ge1\), the argument \(2\pi/p^J\) lies in \((0,2\pi/3]\). Since \(\sin u/u\) decreases on \((0,\pi)\),
\[
\Re\mu_{p,J}\ge c_0:=\frac{\sin(2\pi/3)}{2\pi/3}
=\frac{3\sqrt3}{4\pi}>0.
\]
The prime \(p=2\) can be omitted from this lower bound. Therefore the conditional mean
\[
m_{X,J}:=\mathbb E[\Re F_X(\sigma)\mid C_{X,J}]
=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}\Re\mu_{p,J}
\]
satisfies
\[
m_{X,J}\ge c_0\sum_{3\le p\le X}p^{-\sigma}\longrightarrow\infty. \tag{33.2}
\]
The divergence follows already from \(\sum_p p^{-\sigma}\ge\sum_p1/p=\infty\), since \(\sigma<1\).

Conditional on \(C_{X,J}\), the prime coordinates remain independent. Hence their centered variances add exactly:
\[
\mathbb E\!\left[|F_X-\mathbb E(F_X\mid C_{X,J})|^2\mid C_{X,J}\right]
=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-2\sigma}(1-|\mu_{p,J}|^2)
\le\sum_{p\le X}p^{-2\sigma}
\le\zeta(2\sigma)-1. \tag{33.3}
\]
The last bound is the ordinary integer-lattice Dirichlet sum, equivalently obtained from the Jacobi theta Mellin identity. Applying Chebyshev to the real part yields the explicit concentration bound
\[
\mathbb P\!\left(\Re F_X<\tfrac12m_{X,J}\mid C_{X,J}\right)
\le\frac{4(\zeta(2\sigma)-1)}{m_{X,J}^2}
\longrightarrow0. \tag{33.4}
\]
Thus, conditional on a finite CRT cylinder around the identity, the real prime polynomial is typically large, even though its unconditional Haar variance is bounded. The cylinder's mass is the separate exact factor \(e^{-J\theta(X)}\). This is a direct neighborhood-level refinement of the exceptional-point calculation: it identifies both the size of the hidden prime sum and the precise rarity of the set carrying it.

#### High-precision finite profile

For \(\sigma=3/4\) and \(J=1\), 60-digit arithmetic gives:

The concentration conclusion (33.4) is proved analytically; the table illustrates its finite-scale behavior. The rapidly decreasing cylinder mass explains why this conditional growth is invisible to unconditional mean-square control.

### 5. Source audit

The positive variance bound comes from independent Haar phases and the exact theta/Mellin bound for the ordinary integer lattice. Multiplicativity assigns the coefficients \(p^{-\sigma}\); the p-adic digit cylinder simultaneously determines each conditional phase mean and its probability, which is the precise I-COUPLE step. The sign \(m_{X,J}>0\) is a consequence of the explicit arc integral, not an assumed target positivity. No zero location or target inequality is used. The concentration fact is a scoped conditional-probability theorem, not a new statement about zeta zeros.

### 6. Discrimination test

| world | result | exact step |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Curves over finite fields | No Hasse analogue | There is no Frobenius action or curve degree form in the p-adic prime-coordinate cylinder. |
| (b) Ihara zeta of Ramanujan graphs | No Ramanujan analogue | No graph adjacency or nonbacktracking spectrum occurs. |
| (c) Random Euler products with independent uniform phases | TRUE-TOY RESULT REPRODUCED | On a bounded domain \(D\Subset\{\Re s>1/2\}\), \(\sum_{p\le X}Z_pp^{-s}\) is a Hilbert-valued martingale in \(A^2(D)\) with total increment variance at most \(|D|(\zeta(2\sigma_0)-1)\), \(\sigma_0=\inf_D\Re s>1/2\). It converges almost surely in \(A^2(D)\), hence locally uniformly by Bergman evaluation; the \(k\ge2\) logarithmic Euler terms converge absolutely and locally uniformly. Exponentiating the resulting analytic logarithm proves almost-sure nonvanishing throughout the half-plane. Conditioning finitely many coordinates on a cylinder leaves the independent tail and the same proof intact. |
| (d) Davenport-Heilbronn | Fails I-MULT | There is no canonical prime Euler factorization assigning the independent coordinates \(Z_p\). |
| (e) Beurling generalized primes with bad prime counts | Fails canonical I-LATTICE; externally supplied phases do not discriminate | The ordinary \(\widehat{\mathbb Z}\) digit map, CRT cylinders, and Jacobi theta Mellin identity are not canonical Beurling data. If phases are attached externally to generalized primes, the same random nonvanishing proof and conditional-arc calculation apply whenever the corresponding squared coefficient sum converges; neither detects the deterministic zero geometry. |

### 7. Verdict

**PARTIAL - G0.** The random Euler toy theorem is reproved. The new exact statement is that identity-neighborhood cylinders have conditional prime mean tending to infinity, uniformly bounded conditional variance, and mass exactly \(e^{-J\theta(X)}\). The deterministic transfer remains blocked at the rare-set weighting: the variance estimate is unconditional, while the conditional set has exponentially small mass. No uniform estimate for restoring that mass from the ordinary lattice has been obtained.

### 8. New-idea check

Conditional expectation on p-adic cylinders, independent-coordinate variance addition, Chebyshev's inequality, and the theta Mellin identity are standard. The project-specific result is the explicit joint profile (33.1)-(33.4), including the exact cylinder mass and the lower bound uniform in digit depth \(J\). It strengthens the earlier single-point and Hilbert-evaluation calculations but is not claimed as a new general probability theorem. The random Euler model and its analytic nonvanishing are documented by Lamzouri, Lester, and Radziwiłł, “An effective universality theorem for the Riemann zeta function” (2018), https://arxiv.org/abs/1611.10325.

### 9. Lesson

Replacing one exceptional identity point by a finite CRT neighborhood does not regularize the deterministic prime sum: its conditional mean still diverges. The lost quantity is now explicit in two pieces, a bounded conditional variance and a cylinder mass \(e^{-J\theta(X)}\). A surviving pointwise estimate must explain how the ordinary lattice controls this rare-set weight while retaining the first-power prime scalar; another Haar average or positive approximate identity cannot do so.

### 10. Seed

Seed 29A has completed DEEPEN(3 of 3) and is no longer eligible for deepening. For a fresh pass, test whether the exact cylinder normalization \(e^{J\theta(X)}\) can be realized by an independently evaluable modular/Eisenstein quantity whose prime-character action retains the Euler logarithm and whose analogue fails for bad-zero Beurling systems. First derive its exact finite-cutoff identity and cost; do not assume the normalization is a harmless scalar.

## Pass 34 - PARTIAL under CHANGE-THE-OBJECT: tilt the rare CRT cylinder

### 1. Operator used

**CHANGE-THE-OBJECT.** Replace the cylinder probability alone by a normalized exponential moment of the Euler prime polynomial on that same cylinder. This tests whether its exact lattice normalization can couple to the first-power prime channel in one finite object with a computable local factor and cutoff-uniform error.

### 2. Generate three candidates

**34A - CRT index determinant.** For \(N_X=\prod_{p\le X}p\), use \(N_X^J\mathbb Z\subset\mathbb Z\); its index is the determinant \(N_X^J\), so the zero cylinder has mass \(1/N_X^J\). **I-MULT:** prime factors of \(N_X\) are the local Euler labels. **I-LATTICE:** exact integer-lattice index and CRT quotient. **I-COUPLE:** the same local factors determine the index and coordinate restrictions.

**34B - Modular theta quotient.** For \(\vartheta(t)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb Z}e^{-\pi n^2t}\), represent the normalizer by \(\vartheta(N_X^{2J})/\vartheta(N_X^{-2J})\). Jacobi transformation makes this exactly \(N_X^{-J}\). **I-MULT:** \(N_X\) is the finite prime product. **I-LATTICE:** Jacobi theta transformation. **I-COUPLE:** the prime product is inserted as the lattice scale in the theta identity used to recover its size.

**34C - Cylinder-tilted Euler Laplace transform.** For independent Haar phases \(z_p=e^{2\pi iU_p}\), let \(F_X(\sigma)=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}z_p\), \(C_{X,J}=\{U_p<p^{-J}\ \forall p\le X\}\), and \(\mathscr L_{X,J}=e^{J\theta(X)}\mathbb E[{\bf1}_{C_{X,J}}e^{\operatorname{Re}F_X(\sigma)}]\). Claim: its logarithm is the first-power prime sum plus a uniformly bounded correction; at \(\sigma=1\) it differs from the Mertens product by a convergent local factor. **I-MULT:** unique prime coordinates and Euler coefficients \(p^{-\sigma}\). **I-LATTICE:** exact CRT cylinders and the Jacobi-theta Mellin identity bounding the integer Dirichlet tail. **I-COUPLE:** each prime coordinate sets both its cylinder interval and its Fourier mode in the exponential moment; the exact cylinder normalizer cancels that interval locally.

### 3. Fast kill

- **34A:** \(\det(N_X^J)=N_X^J\) is the primorial already used to define the cylinder; \(\log\det=J\theta(X)\) is a repackaging, not an independent arithmetic evaluation.
- **34B:** Jacobi transformation gives \(\vartheta(N_X^{2J})/\vartheta(N_X^{-2J})=N_X^{-J}\) exactly; the theta identity returns its input scale and adds no independent Euler or spectral constraint.
- **34C:** Develop. Its local integral can control the full exponential moment, improving on the conditional mean/variance profile of Pass 33A.

### 4. Develop 34C: exact local factor and bounded correction

Fix \(\sigma>1/2\), integer \(J\ge1\), and \(X\ge2\). Independence gives \(\mathbb P(C_{X,J})=\prod_{p\le X}p^{-J}=e^{-J\theta(X)}\). Conditioned on the cylinder, each \(U_p\) is uniform on \([0,p^{-J})\). Hence
\[
\mathscr L_{X,J}(\sigma)=\prod_{p\le X}\left(p^J\int_0^{p^{-J}}\exp(p^{-\sigma}\cos(2\pi u))\,du\right).
\]
Put \(w=p^{-J}\), \(a=p^{-\sigma}\), and let \(U\) be uniform on \([0,w]\). The logarithm of the local factor is
\[
a+\log\mathbb E\exp(-a(1-\cos(2\pi U))).
\]
Using \(0\le1-\cos v\le v^2/2\), Jensen's inequality, and \(\mathbb E U^2=w^2/3\), we obtain
\[
-\frac{2\pi^2}{3}aw^2\le
\log\mathbb E e^{-a(1-\cos(2\pi U))}\le0.
\]
Therefore
\[
\boxed{\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}-\frac{2\pi^2}{3}\sum_{p\le X}p^{-(\sigma+2J)}
\le\log\mathscr L_{X,J}(\sigma)\le\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}.}
\]
The error is uniformly bounded in \(X,J\) by \(\frac{2\pi^2}{3}(\zeta(\sigma+2)-1)\). The ordinary integer lattice enters through this absolutely convergent Dirichlet majorant, equivalently the theta Mellin identity
\[
\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2)\zeta(s)=\frac12\int_0^\infty(\vartheta(t)-1)t^{s/2-1}\,dt,\quad
\vartheta(t)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb Z}e^{-\pi n^2t},\quad\Re s>1.
\]
This controls the full tilted moment, not just its conditional mean and variance.

At \(\sigma=1,J=1\), define
\[
A_p=p\int_0^{1/p}e^{p^{-1}\cos(2\pi u)}du,\quad
M_X=\prod_{p\le X}(1-1/p)^{-1},\quad
c_X=\prod_{p\le X}(1-1/p)A_p.
\]
Then \(\mathscr L_{X,1}(1)=c_XM_X\) exactly. Since \(A_p\le e^{1/p}\), \(0<(1-1/p)A_p\le(1-1/p)e^{1/p}<1\). Taylor expansion gives \(\log((1-1/p)A_p)=-1/(2p^2)+O(p^{-3})\), so \(c_X\) decreases to a strictly positive limit by absolute convergence of \(\sum_p p^{-2}\). This is an exact convergent-factor reparameterization, not a new estimate for \(M_X\).

The observed drift is consistent with the proved convergent product; no limit value is extrapolated from three cutoffs.

### 5. Source audit

The positive moment comes from a positive exponential integrated against Haar measure. The normalizer is the exact CRT cardinality, and theta/Mellin bounds only the convergent squared-scale remainder. Multiplicativity and lattice exactness meet inside the same local integral. No positivity of \(Q\), Robin inequality, zero location, or target-strength estimate is assumed. However, no map from \(\mathscr L_{X,J}\) to the deterministic functional \(Q\) has been derived. At \(\sigma=1\), \(\mathscr L=c_XM_X\) is only a finite reparameterization and gives no sign for \(M_X-e^\gamma\log\theta(X)\).

### 6. Discrimination test

| world | result | exact step |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Curves over finite fields | No Hasse analogue | No curve, Frobenius eigenvalue, or degree pairing occurs. |
| (b) Ihara zeta of Ramanujan graphs | No Ramanujan analogue | No adjacency or nonbacktracking operator occurs. |
| (c) Random Euler products with independent uniform phases | TRUE-TOY RESULT REPRODUCED | On \(D\Subset\{\Re s>1/2\}\), \(S_X(s)=\sum_{p\le X}Z_pp^{-s}\) is a Hilbert-valued martingale in \(A^2(D)\), with total increment variance at most \(|D|(\zeta(2\sigma_0)-1)\), \(\sigma_0=\inf_D\Re s>1/2\). Almost-sure \(A^2\) convergence implies local uniform convergence by Bergman point evaluation. The higher Euler-log terms converge absolutely since \(\sum_p p^{-2\sigma_0}/(1-p^{-\sigma_0})<\infty\). Thus the analytic logarithm converges locally uniformly and exponentiation proves \(\prod_p(1-Z_pp^{-s})^{-1}\ne0\) almost surely for \(\Re s>1/2\); the cylinder tilt conditions finitely many coordinates of this same phase model. |
| (d) Davenport–Heilbronn | Fails I-MULT | No canonical Euler factorization supplies the prime-indexed \(z_p\) and \(p^{-\sigma}\) modes. |
| (e) Beurling generalized primes with regular integer counts and bad prime counts | Fails canonical I-LATTICE | The construction uses ordinary rings \(\mathbb Z/N_X^J\mathbb Z\), their CRT decomposition into \(p\)-adic digits, and ordinary theta/Mellin data; generalized primes supply no such canonical prime-indexed residue rings. If phases are assigned externally, the same random calculation survives and does not constrain their deterministic bad zeros. |

The random-Euler theorem is reproved, but the statistic is not zero-selective: its externally phased analogue survives without information about deterministic zeros.

### 7. Verdict

**PARTIAL — G0.** The exact finite-cylinder Laplace product and its uniform correction bound are proved, and the random-Euler nonvanishing theorem is reproduced. At \(\sigma=1\), the exact factorization is \(\mathscr L_{X,1}(1)=c_XM_X\) with \(c_X\downarrow c_\infty>0\). No inequality for \(Q\), new Mertens-product bound, or zero-selective spectral object follows.

### 8. New-idea check

CRT cylinders, independent-phase martingales, theta Mellin identities, and exponential tilting are standard. Pass 33A records the cylinder mass and conditional mean/variance, but not this normalized exponential-moment product or its exact \(c_XM_X\) factorization. This is a project-level extension, not a claim of priority; no external novelty claim is made. The random-Euler nonvanishing argument is standard and is not claimed as new.

### 9. Lesson

The rare-cylinder normalizer cancels exactly inside a local exponential moment, leaving the first-power prime sum plus a uniformly summable correction. At \(\sigma=1\) this is only a convergent-factor reparameterization of the Mertens product. A next candidate must produce a signed or spectral constraint on the finite correction beyond algebraic rewriting, and must lose that constraint in bad-zero Beurling systems.

### 10. Seed

Seed 34C: find an independent lattice or modular representation of
\[
\log c_X=\sum_{p\le X}\log\!\left((1-1/p)\,p\int_0^{1/p}e^{p^{-1}\cos(2\pi u)}du\right)
\]
with a one-sided remainder strong enough to constrain \(M_X-e^\gamma\log\theta(X)\), and which fails canonically for bad-zero Beurling systems. Reject any argument whose only content is \(\mathscr L_{X,1}(1)=c_XM_X\).



## Pass 35 - PARTIAL under IMPORT: relative entropy budget for a rare prime cylinder

### 1. Operator used

**IMPORT.** Import the Donsker–Varadhan entropy variational inequality and Hoeffding's sub-Gaussian bound into the rare-cylinder model. Test whether information cost alone can control the conditional first-power prime drift that Pass 34's exact tilted moment exposes.

### 2. Generate three candidates

**35A - KL/Hoeffding cylinder bound.** On \(\Omega_X=\prod_{p\le X}\mathbb T\) with product Haar measure, compare the conditioned law \(\nu_{X,J}=\mu(\,\cdot\,|C_{X,J})\) to Haar using \(D(\nu\Vert\mu)=J\theta(X)\), and apply entropy duality to \(F_X(\sigma)=\sum p^{-\sigma}\operatorname{Re}z_p\). Claim: the exact CRT information cost and theta-controlled variance force a conditional drift smaller than the observed prime mean. **I-MULT:** each coordinate and coefficient \(p^{-\sigma}\) comes from the Euler prime factors. **I-LATTICE:** CRT gives the cylinder mass, and Jacobi theta/Mellin gives \(\sum_p p^{-2\sigma}\le\zeta(2\sigma)-1\). **I-COUPLE:** the same p-coordinate fixes the entropy cost and the summand whose variance is bounded by the ordinary lattice.

**35B - Product-torus logarithmic Sobolev transfer.** Apply the tensorized log-Sobolev inequality to the same product torus and the Lipschitz observable \(F_X\); claim the entropy cost \(J\theta(X)\) yields a sharper conditional drift than the KL/Hoeffding estimate. **I-MULT:** prime-indexed Euler coordinates. **I-LATTICE:** exact CRT cylinder and theta Mellin variance bound. **I-COUPLE:** coordinate-wise gradients of the same Euler observable are summed against the same local cylinder entropy.

**35C - Nested-cylinder entropy decrement.** Compare \(C_{X,J}\) and \(C_{X,J+1}\), whose relative entropy increment is exactly \(\theta(X)\), and use the change in the conditional prime mean as an entropy decrement. Claim: some digit depth makes the entropy cost small while retaining a nonzero prime-selective mean. **I-MULT:** one local Euler mode per prime. **I-LATTICE:** the nested p-adic CRT cylinders and the theta/Mellin tail. **I-COUPLE:** the same local arc width determines both the information increment and the change in that prime mode's conditional mean.

### 3. Fast kill

- **35B:** For a linear sum of independent bounded coordinates, the tensor log-Sobolev/Herbst route returns the same square-root entropy scale as the KL/Hoeffding variational inequality; it cannot improve the relevant order without additional structure.
- **35C:** The entropy increment is \(\theta(X)\) at every digit depth, while the conditional mean increment is bounded by a convergent sum of order \(\sum_p p^{-(\sigma+2J)}\). Increasing depth makes the mean change smaller but never lowers the information price.
- **35A:** Develop and compare its rigorous upper bound with the exact conditional mean.

### 4. Develop 35A: exact entropy inequality and scale comparison

Let \(\mu_X\) be product Haar measure on \(\Omega_X\), \(C=C_{X,J}\), and \(\nu=\mu_X(\cdot\mid C)\). Since \(\mu_X(C)=e^{-J\theta(X)}\),
\[
D(\nu\Vert\mu_X)=\int\log\frac{d\nu}{d\mu_X}\,d\nu
=\log\frac1{\mu_X(C)}=J\theta(X).
\]
For \(Y=F_X(\sigma)\), each centered summand \(p^{-\sigma}\operatorname{Re}z_p\) has mean zero and lies in \([-p^{-\sigma},p^{-\sigma}]\). Hoeffding's lemma and independence imply
\[
\log\mathbb E_{\mu_X}e^{\lambda Y}
\le\frac{\lambda^2}{2}V_X(\sigma),\qquad
V_X(\sigma)=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-2\sigma}.
\]
The Donsker–Varadhan variational inequality therefore gives, for every \(\lambda>0\),
\[
\mathbb E_\nu Y\le\frac{J\theta(X)}{\lambda}+\frac{\lambda V_X(\sigma)}2.
\]
Optimizing in \(\lambda\) yields the exact estimate
\[
\boxed{\ \mathbb E[\operatorname{Re}F_X(\sigma)\mid C_{X,J}]
\le\sqrt{2J\theta(X)V_X(\sigma)}
\le\sqrt{2J\theta(X)(\zeta(2\sigma)-1)}.\ }
\]
The same expectation is explicitly
\[
m_{X,J}=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}
\frac{\sin(2\pi/p^J)}{2\pi/p^J}
\ge \frac{3\sqrt3}{4\pi}\sum_{3\le p\le X}p^{-\sigma}.
\]
Thus the inequality is valid, but its scale does not constrain the observed mean. For fixed \(1/2<\sigma<1\) and fixed \(J\), the prime number theorem and \(\theta(X)\sim X\) give
\[
m_{X,J}\sim\frac{X^{1-\sigma}}{(1-\sigma)\log X},\qquad
\sqrt{2J\theta(X)V_X(\sigma)}\asymp\sqrt X.
\]
Consequently their ratio tends to zero like \(X^{1/2-\sigma}/\log X\): the information-theoretic allowance is asymptotically larger than the actual conditional drift. At \(\sigma=1\), the mean is of order \(\log\log X\), also much smaller than the \(O(\sqrt X)\) entropy allowance. Hence this imported inequality cannot rule out the rare-cylinder concentration, even though it rigorously bounds every admissible conditional law by its entropy.

For the true random-Euler toy, the underlying phase model still proves almost-sure nonvanishing. On a bounded domain \(D\Subset\{\Re s>1/2\}\), the sums \(\sum_{p\le X}Z_pp^{-s}\) form a Hilbert-valued martingale in \(A^2(D)\), with total increment variance at most \(|D|(\zeta(2\sigma_0)-1)\), \(\sigma_0=\inf_D\Re s\). Martingale convergence gives almost-sure \(A^2(D)\) convergence and hence locally uniform convergence on compact subsets. The higher Euler-log terms converge absolutely and locally uniformly because \(\sum_p p^{-2\sigma_0}/(1-p^{-\sigma_0})<\infty\). The analytic logarithm therefore converges locally uniformly, and exponentiation proves the random Euler product is nonzero on \(\Re s>1/2\). This reproduces the toy theorem; the entropy bound itself adds no pointwise deterministic estimate.

### 5. Source audit

The estimate comes from the general entropy variational inequality and Hoeffding's lemma, not from a target assumption. The lattice contribution is the exact theta/Mellin representation of the convergent Dirichlet majorant; the arithmetic contribution is the prime-coordinate Euler sum. Their coupling is real, but the relative-entropy price \(J\theta(X)\) is too large for the bound to constrain the conditional mean. No Weil-form sign, Robin inequality, or zero location has been used. The argument is not a proof of the target and supplies no deterministic comparison between the random phase law and the integer-prime configuration.

### 7. Verdict

The exact asymptotic comparison shows the ceiling is much looser than the rare-cylinder mean for every fixed \(\sigma>1/2\); thus this route does not control the deterministic first-power prime drift or the target inequality.

### 8. New-idea check

Donsker–Varadhan entropy duality, Hoeffding concentration, product-torus tensorization, and Bergman martingale convergence are standard. Pass 34 developed a local tilted Laplace product; this pass instead tests the independent information-theoretic cost of conditioning and proves a precise asymptotic mismatch. The new project result is only this quantified failure for the specified rare-cylinder prime observable; no general entropy no-go or literature-priority claim is made.

### 9. Lesson

The exact relative-entropy cost of the zero-digit cylinder grows on the \(\theta(X)\) scale, so a square-root entropy bound is far above the prime drift whenever \(\sigma>1/2\). Changing from conditional variance to standard entropy concentration does not recover a deterministic value. A viable imported inequality must exploit a signed arithmetic observable or a spectral constraint whose information cost is tied to the actual prime correction, not just the event's logarithmic rarity.

### 10. Seed

Seed 35A: seek an imported signed/spectral inequality that controls the finite correction \(c_X\) from Pass 34 directly, with a one-sided remainder comparable to the shrinking difference between the Mertens product and \(e^\gamma\log\theta(X)\), and whose canonical form is unavailable for bad-zero Beurling systems. Reject another KL, log-Sobolev, or unconditional-variance bound with a \(\sqrt{\theta(X)}\) allowance.

## Pass 36 — DEEPEN(1 of 3), DEAD below G0 under INVARIANT: exact entropy envelope saturates the phase maximum

### 1. Operator used

**INVARIANT.** Keep the relative-entropy budget fixed and replace the Hoeffding surrogate from Pass 35 with the exact cumulant-generating function of the prime-circle coordinates. The invariant under test is the information budget (D_X=J\theta(X)); the question is whether exact variational optimization preserves the actual CRT cylinder's local deficit.

### 2. Generate three candidates

**36A — Exact Bessel-Cramér envelope.** On \(\Omega_X=\mathbb T^{\pi(X)}\) with product Haar \(\mu_X\), set \(F_X=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}\Re z_p\), \(S_X=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}\), and \(B_X(D)=\sup_{D(\nu\Vert\mu_X)\le D}\mathbb E_\nu F_X\). Its exact dual is \(B_X(D)=\inf_{\lambda>0}(D+\sum_{p\le X}\log I_0(\lambda p^{-\sigma}))/\lambda\); claim: at \(D=J\theta(X)\), this envelope determines the actual cylinder mean to \(o(1)\). **I-MULT:** prime Euler coordinates and weights. **I-LATTICE:** exact CRT entropy and the theta/Mellin prime-square majorant. **I-COUPLE:** the same prime coordinates occur in the local Bessel cumulant and the CRT information cost.

**36B — Chain-rule allocation.** Decompose the KL budget into its prime-coordinate conditional entropies and optimize a separate exponential parameter at each prime; claim: the chain rule recovers the cylinder's prime-specific mean without paying for the scalar envelope's worst allocation. **I-MULT:** one Euler factor per coordinate. **I-LATTICE:** the CRT factorization and \(\theta(X)\). **I-COUPLE:** each coordinate's exact relative entropy is paired with that coordinate's Euler coefficient and circle cumulant.

**36C — Prime-statistic data processing.** Push both the conditioned and Haar laws through the vector of prime-real-part sums grouped by logarithmic intervals, then use the resulting relative-entropy contraction with theta/Mellin bounds on each interval. Claim: the grouped statistic retains the local cylinder deficit while reducing the entropy allowance. **I-MULT:** groups are indexed by Euler primes. **I-LATTICE:** logarithmic bins and the exact integer theta majorant. **I-COUPLE:** the statistic groups the same prime phases that generate the CRT entropy.

### 3. Fast kill

- **36B:** The chain rule is an exact decomposition of the same KL constraint; without retaining the full coordinate laws it gives the same separable Bessel variational problem as 36A. Retaining those laws simply inserts the known local conditioning and restates the cylinder integral.
- **36C:** Data processing can only decrease relative entropy and supplies no new signed constraint on the image law; externally assigning the same prime phases to a false-zero Euler system preserves the grouped estimate. It therefore fails false-world discrimination.

### 4. Develop 36A: exact dual and its asymptotic degeneration

For \(a_p=p^{-\sigma}\), independence gives the exact log moment generating function
\[
K_X(\lambda)=\log\mathbb E_{\mu_X}e^{\lambda F_X}
=\sum_{p\le X}\log I_0(\lambda a_p),
\qquad I_0(t)=\frac1{2\pi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}e^{t\cos u}\,du.
\]
The entropy variational inequality gives \(B_X(D)=\inf_{\lambda>0}(D+K_X(\lambda))/\lambda\). At an interior minimizer, \(\lambda K_X'(\lambda)-K_X(\lambda)=D\), and \(B_X(D)=K_X'(\lambda)\). The exact CRT-conditioned mean is
\[
m_{X,J}=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}\operatorname{sinc}(2\pi/p^J),
\quad \operatorname{sinc}(u)=\sin(u)/u,
\]
while \(D(\mu_X(\cdot\mid C_{X,J})\Vert\mu_X)=J\theta(X)\).

The decisive comparison is not a numerical extrapolation. Put \(n_X=\pi(X)\), \(D_X=J\theta(X)\), and \(\delta_X=e^{-D_X/n_X}\). Let \(\nu_X\) be the product law that is uniform on the normalized circle arc \([-\delta_X/2,\delta_X/2]\) in every coordinate. Then
\[
D(\nu_X\Vert\mu_X)=n_X\log(1/\delta_X)=D_X,
\qquad
\mathbb E_{\nu_X}F_X=\operatorname{sinc}(\pi\delta_X)S_X.
\]
Since \(1-\operatorname{sinc}(u)\le u^2/6\),
\[
0\le S_X-B_X(D_X)
\le \frac{\pi^2}{6}\delta_X^2S_X.
\]
By the prime number theorem, \(\theta(X)/\pi(X)=\log X-1+o(1)\), so \(\delta_X=e^{J+o(1)}X^{-J}\). For every fixed \(\sigma\in(1/2,1]\), \(S_X=O(X^{1-\sigma}/\log X)\) when \(\sigma<1\), and \(S_X=O(\log\log X)\) when \(\sigma=1\). Thus \(S_X-B_X(D_X)\to0\): the optimal scalar KL envelope approaches the coordinatewise maximum.

In contrast,
\[
S_X-m_{X,J}=C_X
=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}\bigl(1-\operatorname{sinc}(2\pi/p^J)\bigr)
\longrightarrow C_{\sigma,J}>0.
\]
The series converges because \(1-\operatorname{sinc}(u)\le u^2/6\) and \(\sum_p p^{-(\sigma+2J)}<\infty\); it is strictly positive already at \(p=2\). Since the actual conditioned law is admissible in the variational supremum, \(B_X(D_X)\ge m_{X,J}\). Consequently
\[
\boxed{B_X(D_X)-m_{X,J}\longrightarrow C_{\sigma,J}>0.}
\]
This proves a precise obstruction: optimizing the scalar entropy inequality cannot recover the cylinder's mean to \(o(1)\). Its admissible laws spend the same entropy budget by concentrating all phase coordinates into arcs of width approximately \(e^J/X^J\), erasing the different low-prime arc widths of the actual cylinder.

The entropy variational formula, Bessel cumulant, product-arc relative entropy, and prime number theorem asymptotics are standard ingredients. The entropy cost \(J\theta(X)\) is large enough to let the scalar variational problem concentrate every coordinate almost maximally: its optimized upper bound tends to \(S_X\), while the true CRT arc law loses the positive constant \(C_{\sigma,J}\). ## Pass 37 — DEEPEN(2 of 3), DEAD below G0 under CHANGE-THE-OBJECT: primewise entropy still leaves constant slack

### 1. Operator used

**CHANGE-THE-OBJECT.** Replace the single global relative-entropy constraint by a separate entropy budget at each prime coordinate. This prevents the global optimizer from moving information freely between primes; test whether the stronger arithmetic allocation recovers the exact local cylinder contribution.

### 2. Generate three candidates

**37A — Primewise Gibbs caps.** For each prime (p\le X), maximize (\mathbb E\Re z_p) among circle laws of relative entropy at most (J\log p), and sum the maxima with Euler weights (p^{-\sigma}). Claim: the product of these local bounds determines the exact CRT-conditioned prime sum up to (o(1)). **I-MULT:** unique factorization supplies a separate Euler coordinate and coefficient (p^{-\sigma}) for each prime. **I-LATTICE:** the (p)-adic CRT cylinder has exact local mass (p^{-J}); Jacobi theta Mellin gives (\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2)\zeta(s)=\frac12\int_0^\infty(\theta(t)-1)t^{s/2-1}dt), controlling the convergent prime tail. **I-COUPLE:** each coordinate’s exact CRT information cost (J\log p) is paired with its own Euler coefficient and circle cumulant.

**37B — Exact marginal-arc constraints.** Constrain each prime marginal to the CRT arc (A_{p,J}) and compute the weighted mean. Claim: the resulting positive sum supplies an independent bound on the deterministic prime drift. **I-MULT:** one factor per prime. **I-LATTICE:** the exact local CRT arcs and theta/Mellin tail. **I-COUPLE:** the same local interval defines both the prime phase mean and its arithmetic weight.

**37C — Primewise KL plus zero cross-covariance.** Impose the 37A marginal entropy budgets and the CRT covariance identities (\mathbb E[(\Re z_p-m_p)(\Re z_q-m_q)]=0) for (p\ne q), then form a positive covariance Gram. Claim: the Gram remainder forces a signed correction absent from scalar entropy. **I-MULT:** Euler prime coordinates. **I-LATTICE:** CRT independence and theta/Mellin control of diagonal tails. **I-COUPLE:** exact CRT cross-covariance constraints are imposed on the same prime coordinates as the Euler-weighted Gram.

### 3. Fast kill

- **37B:** Fixing each marginal to the actual CRT arc makes its mean the already-known sinc integral; the claimed conclusion is exactly the definition of the conditional mean, with no independent inequality.
- **37C:** The product Haar law also has zero cross-covariances, so these constraints hold in a phase model with arbitrary deterministic zero data; the Gram positivity is not zero-selective.

### 4. Develop 37A: local Gibbs extremizers and the irreducible positive slack

Write normalized circle measure as (du) on ([-1/2,1/2)). For (d>0), define
[
\beta(d)=\sup_{D(\nu\Vert du)\le d}\int\cos(2\pi u)\,d\nu(u).
]
The Gibbs variational principle gives the exact one-coordinate formula
[
\beta(d)=\frac{I_1(\lambda)}{I_0(\lambda)},\qquad
\lambda\frac{I_1(\lambda)}{I_0(\lambda)}-\log I_0(\lambda)=d,
]
where (I_0(\lambda)=\int e^{\lambda\cos(2\pi u)}du) and (I_1=I_0'). The entropy equation has a unique positive solution: its left side increases continuously from zero to infinity.

On the actual CRT cylinder (C_{X,J}), the (p)-coordinate is uniform on an arc of normalized length (p^{-J}). Its relative entropy is (J\log p), and its mean is
[
m_p=\operatorname{sinc}(2\pi/p^J).
]
Therefore the primewise cap gives
[
m_{X,J}(\sigma)\le U_X(\sigma,J)
=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}\beta(J\log p).
]
The inequality is strict in each coordinate: the entropy-maximizing Gibbs density is smooth and proportional to (e^{\lambda\cos(2\pi u)}), whereas the CRT law is uniform on a proper arc.

The excess has an exact monotone limit:
[
\Delta_X=U_X-m_{X,J}
=\sum_{p\le X}p^{-\sigma}
\left[\beta(J\log p)-\operatorname{sinc}(2\pi/p^J)\right]
\uparrow\Delta_{\sigma,J}.
]
Every summand is strictly positive, and
[
0<\beta(J\log p)-\operatorname{sinc}(2\pi/p^J)
\le 1-\operatorname{sinc}(2\pi/p^J)
\le \frac{2\pi^2}{3p^{2J}}.
]
The last bound follows by integrating (1-\cos t\le t^2/2). Thus \(\Delta_{\sigma,J}<\infty\) whenever \(\sigma+2J>1\), and \(\Delta_{\sigma,J}>0\); for \(\sigma=3/4,J=1\), the (p=2) term alone is
[
2^{-3/4}\beta(\log2)=0.447945730330195997865882602667729332144133044.
]
So the primewise entropy refinement has a nonvanishing additive slack, rather than the (o(1)) precision claimed.

**DEAD — below G0.** The exact primewise KL cap is a valid bound, but its excess over the true cylinder mean converges to a strictly positive constant. It neither rederives the random-Euler result by its own mechanism nor rejects externally phased bad-zero systems. This is a scoped limitation of marginal-entropy relaxations, not a no-go for signed joint constraints.

### 8. New-idea check

The Gibbs variational principle, modified Bessel functions, and circle concentration are standard. Pass 36 showed that a global entropy budget lets the optimizer redistribute information and saturate the coordinatewise maximum; Pass 37 fixes each prime's budget and proves the remaining slack is a positive convergent prime series. This project-specific comparison is not claimed as a new general theorem or a literature-priority result.

### 9. Lesson

Primewise information prevents global redistribution but still compares the actual uniform CRT arc with a strictly more efficient Gibbs density at every prime. The resulting local errors decay fast enough to be summable, yet their sum is a positive constant, too coarse to remove the target's signed correction. A stronger candidate must constrain the shape of each local law or couple two or more primes through a signed lattice identity; another entropy cap is not enough.

### 10. Seed

Seed 37A: add an independently justified signed pair or higher correlation constraint to the primewise Gibbs variational problem, derived from an exact theta/Poisson identity and unavailable in externally phased bad-zero systems. Require that the constraint reduce the positive limiting Gibbs slack to zero without simply prescribing the CRT arc marginals.

## Pass 38 — DEEPEN(3 of 3), DEAD below G0 under IMPORT: pair moments encode the local cylinder

### 1. Operator used

**IMPORT.** Import the degree-two truncated moment-matrix method: use pair moments as semidefinite constraints on the prime-coordinate laws, rather than another scalar or primewise entropy cap. Test whether exact lattice pair data force a nontrivial signed bound on the weighted prime mean.

### 2. Generate three candidates

**38A — CRT pair-moment pinning.** For (p\le X), let (x_p=\mathbb E\Re z_p) under a product phase law, and impose (x_px_q=m_pm_q) for every distinct pair, where (m_p=\operatorname{sinc}(2\pi/p^J)) is the exact CRT arc mean. Claim: the degree-two pair-moment constraints alone force (\sum p^{-\sigma}x_p=\sum p^{-\sigma}m_p). **I-MULT:** prime Euler coordinates and their (p^{-\sigma}) weights. **I-LATTICE:** exact CRT factorization gives the pair moments; Jacobi theta Mellin bounds the convergent prime tail. **I-COUPLE:** the same prime-pair coordinates carry both their multiplicative Euler weights and exact lattice arc moments.

**38B — Centered covariance PSD constraint.** Impose the CRT condition (\operatorname{Cov}(\Re z_p,\Re z_q)=0) for distinct primes and use positivity of the covariance matrix to improve the primewise Gibbs bound. Claim: cross-coordinate PSD forces the local entropy slack to cancel. **I-MULT:** one variable per Euler prime. **I-LATTICE:** CRT independence and theta Mellin for the diagonal sum. **I-COUPLE:** the covariance matrix is built from the same prime variables and lattice law.

**38C — Poisson-filtered prime-pair Gram.** Use the exact finite identity (\sum_{a\bmod q}e^{2\pi ia(r-s)/q}=q\mathbf1_{r\equiv s\bmod q}) to filter a prime-pair Gram by residue classes, then pair the result with the primewise entropy budget. Claim: the lattice projector supplies a signed pair correction. **I-MULT:** Euler coefficients (p^{-\sigma}) label the prime entries. **I-LATTICE:** finite Poisson/root-of-unity orthogonality on \(\mathbb Z/q\mathbb Z\). **I-COUPLE:** the projector acts on the very prime-pair Gram weighted by the Euler coefficients.

### 3. Fast kill

- **38B:** Independent product Gibbs laws also have zero centered cross-covariance for arbitrary coordinate means, so the covariance constraint leaves the marginal optimization unchanged and does not remove the slack.
- **38C:** Root-of-unity orthogonality retains a congruence-filtered two-prime Gram, not a signed one-prime Euler term; this repeats the character-filter/ratio-correlation mismatch, and the same projector can be applied to externally supplied bad-zero phase labels.

### 4. Develop 38A: what exact pair moments imply

Fix (J=1), (X\ge7), and the primes (2,3,5,7\). Under the CRT-conditioned product law,
[
m_2=\operatorname{sinc}(\pi)=0,\quad
m_3=\operatorname{sinc}(2\pi/3),\quad
m_5=\operatorname{sinc}(2\pi/5),\quad
m_7=\operatorname{sinc}(2\pi/7).
]
For any product candidate law, its pair expectations factor:
[
\mathbb E[(\Re z_p)(\Re z_q)]=x_px_q.
]
If these are fixed to the exact CRT values (m_pm_q), then the three positive constraints among (3,5,7) imply
[
(x_3/m_3)(x_5/m_5)=(x_3/m_3)(x_7/m_7)=(x_5/m_5)(x_7/m_7)=1.
]
Thus the three ratios are either all (+1) or all (-1). The constraints involving (2) give (x_2x_3=x_2x_5=x_2x_7=0), hence (x_2=0). Maximizing the positive weighted objective selects the positive branch, so the upper envelope equals the known CRT mean on these coordinates.

That equality does not come from a new inequality: the imposed pair data are exactly the pair expectations of the already specified CRT-conditioned law. They encode its local means. In particular, no arithmetic argument has derived these pair constraints from the Weil functional or from a theta/Poisson identity independent of the local arc integrals.

#### High-precision finite data

At 70-digit working precision, the local means and exact pair products are:

| (m_3) | 0.4134966715663440371334948737347270810480398602749804895995245152182727 |
