These products are exact finite CRT moments up to the displayed numerical evaluation. Their algebraic pinning of the mean is immediate once they are imposed; the 70-digit calculation is a consistency check, not evidence for an independent inequality.

### 5. Source audit

The moment-matrix positivity is generic. The only exact pinning comes from inserting (m_pm_q), which already contains the arc means sought; this is data encoding, not an independently derived sign or bound. The theta Mellin identity
[
\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2)\zeta(s)=\frac12\int_0^\infty(\theta(t)-1)t^{s/2-1}\,dt
]
can bound the convergent tail (sum_p p^{-(\sigma+2J)}), but it supplies none of the imposed pair values. Nothing equivalent to the target is assumed, yet no target-relevant inequality follows.

### 6. Discrimination test

| world | result | exact step |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Curves over finite fields | No Hasse analogue | The degree-two phase moments do not construct a curve, Frobenius action, or eigenvalue estimate. |
| (b) Ihara zeta of Ramanujan graphs | No Ramanujan analogue | No graph or nonbacktracking spectrum is produced. |
| (c) Random Euler products with independent uniform phases | The standard nonvanishing theorem is reproved in the same phase space, but not by the pair-moment pinning | On bounded (D\Subset\{\Re s>1/2\}), partial logarithms (\sum_{p\le X}Z_pp^{-s}) are Hilbert-valued martingales in (A^2(D)) with total increment variance at most (|D|(\zeta(2\sigma_0)-1)), \(\sigma_0=\inf_D\Re s>1/2\). Almost-sure (A^2) convergence plus Bergman point evaluation gives local uniform convergence; higher logarithmic powers converge absolutely by \(\sum_p p^{-2\sigma_0}/(1-p^{-\sigma_0})<\infty\). Exponentiating the analytic logarithm proves almost-sure nonvanishing. The moment-pinning step itself is absent from this proof and does not earn G0. |
| (d) Davenport–Heilbronn | Fails I-MULT canonically | There are no Euler prime factors defining these local variables or coefficients. |
| (e) Beurling generalized primes with regular integer counts and bad prime counts | Fails I-LATTICE canonically; externally assigned phase version is nondiscriminating | Ordinary CRT arc moments and the integer theta transform are absent. If the same prime-coordinate arcs and pair moments are artificially supplied, the algebraic pinning works unchanged regardless of the deterministic zero set. |

The candidate fails the false-world test and the required same-mechanism toy reproduction: its only successful step is an imposed moment identity, while generic phase models retain the same moment optimization.

### 7. Verdict

**DEAD — below G0.** Exact pair-moment constraints force the finite local mean, but only because the desired CRT moments were supplied as constraints. No independently derived signed arithmetic inequality appears; the construction neither derives its random-Euler toy theorem nor rejects externally supplied phases on a bad-zero system. This is a scoped diagnosis of degree-two moment pinning, not a no-go for other joint constraints.

### 8. New-idea check

Truncated moment matrices, pairwise moment factorization for product laws, and root-of-unity orthogonality are standard. Pass 37's primewise Gibbs caps left a positive local slack; Pass 38 shows that removing it by exact pair moments simply encodes the target conditional marginals. No novelty or priority claim is made.

### 9. Lesson

Joint constraints help only if they are derived independently from multiplicativity and the integer lattice. Exact moments can force the desired local mean, but when those moments are inserted from the conditioned arc law the argument is tautological at the model level and remains phase-generic at the zero level. The exhausted deepening line must now seek a signed arithmetic identity that produces its pair constraint rather than prescribe it.

### 10. Seed

Seed 38A: derive a prime-pair signed moment from one explicit Chebyshev, theta, or Poisson identity and show that it is not implied by local CRT arc conditioning or independent Haar phases; only then place it in a moment matrix. Reject any constraint whose numerical right-hand side is computed from the same local law whose mean it is supposed to certify.


## Pass 39 — FRESH under RISING-SEA: factorial divisor cocycle and shifted CRT Gram

### 1. Operator

**RISING-SEA.** Let the integer scale grow through the factorial lattice, but keep the prime-power divisor labels attached to each increment. The test is whether this scale limit turns an exact lattice identity into a signed prime-pair constraint that is independent of the target functional.

### 2. Generate three candidates

**39A — Shifted Chebyshev-cocycle Gram (developed).** Define \(F(X)=\sum_{d\le X}\Lambda(d)\lfloor X/d\rfloor\), and for fixed \(H,r,s\ge0\) define \(G_{r,s}(X,H)=\sum_{j=0}^{H-1}\log(X+j+r)\log(X+j+s)\). Claim: the CRT expansion of this positive Gram yields an independently controlled signed prime-pair correction to the linear Weil prime term. **I-MULT:** \(\log n=\sum_{d\mid n}\Lambda(d)\). **I-LATTICE:** exact floor counts and simultaneous congruence counts on \(\mathbb Z\). **I-COUPLE:** the same \(\Lambda(d)\) divisor expansion creates both factors in \(G\), while the integer shifts give the CRT compatibility condition.

**39B — Fourier transform of divisor-jump increments.** For \(q\ge2\), set \(a_X(j)=\sum_{d\mid X+j}\Lambda(d)=\log(X+j)\), and form its length-\(H\) discrete Fourier energy \(\sum_{r\bmod q}|\sum_{j<H}a_X(j)e^{2\pi i rj/q}|^2\). Claim: a nonzero Fourier mode isolates prime-power divisor classes with a signed correction. **I-MULT:** von Mangoldt divisor identity. **I-LATTICE:** exact roots-of-unity orthogonality on integer intervals. **I-COUPLE:** divisor labels enter the Fourier coefficients through the same shifted divisibility indicators.

**39C — Randomized factorial Euler martingale.** Put independent Haar phases on prime coordinates of the factorial-divisor expansion and use the theta/Mellin identity to bound the square-summable tail of the random Euler logarithm. Claim: this coupled random object proves almost-sure nonvanishing and supplies a deterministic lattice inequality by conditioning at the identity. **I-MULT:** unique factorization defines the independent Euler coordinates. **I-LATTICE:** the Jacobi theta/Mellin identity bounds the prime-square tail. **I-COUPLE:** the same prime coefficients appear in the randomized logarithm and its lattice variance.

### 3. Fast kill

- **39B:** Parseval gives a nonnegative sum of Fourier squares, but expansion returns congruence-filtered divisor-pair correlations; it does not produce a one-index \(\Lambda(n)\) sample, and the same PSD identity works for arbitrary supplied coefficients.
- **39C:** The martingale nonvanishing proof is already the Pass 26 mechanism; conditioning at a Haar-null identity is not justified by the variance bound, and the same random-phase theorem can be attached externally to bad-zero systems.

### 4. Develop 39A: exact identity and its limit

For every integer \(n\ge1\), the prime-power divisor identity is
\[
\sum_{d\mid n}\Lambda(d)=\log n.
\]
Therefore, by changing the order of a finite sum,
\[
F(X)=\sum_{d\le X}\Lambda(d)\left\lfloor\frac Xd\right\rfloor
=\sum_{n\le X}\sum_{d\mid n}\Lambda(d)
=\sum_{n\le X}\log n
=\log(X!).
\]
Taking a first difference yields \(F(X)-F(X-1)=\log X\); taking a second difference yields
\[
F(X)-2F(X-1)+F(X-2)=\log\frac{X}{X-1}.
\]
These are exact, but their evaluation is the factorial identity itself. No prime error term is gained by treating \(F\) as a new lattice observable.

The proposed positive object is the Gram matrix
\[
G_{r,s}(X,H)=\sum_{j=0}^{H-1}\ell(X+j+r)\ell(X+j+s),
\qquad \ell(n)=\log n.
\]
For any real coefficients \(c_r\),
\[
\sum_{r,s}c_rG_{r,s}c_s
=\sum_{j=0}^{H-1}\left(\sum_r c_r\log(X+j+r)\right)^2\ge0.
\]
Expanding both logarithms by \(\ell(n)=\sum_{d\mid n}\Lambda(d)\) gives
\[
G_{r,s}(X,H)=
\sum_{d,e}\Lambda(d)\Lambda(e)\,
\#\{0\le j<H:d\mid X+j+r,\ e\mid X+j+s\}.
\]
The congruences are compatible exactly when \(\gcd(d,e)\mid(r-s)\). If compatible, their solutions form one residue class modulo \(\operatorname{lcm}(d,e)\), so the count is \(H/\operatorname{lcm}(d,e)+O(1)\), with absolute error at most 1. Thus multiplicativity and the integer lattice do interact in one exact formula, but the output is a **two-index quadratic correlation**. The target Weil functional has a linear prime-power distribution, sampling \(g(\log n)\) with coefficient \(\Lambda(n)/\sqrt n\); no derived identity converts this Gram into those one-point samples or controls the pole and archimedean terms.

#### High-precision finite diagnostic

Directly summing \(\Lambda(d)\lfloor X/d\rfloor\) and independently evaluating \(\log\Gamma(X+1)\) at 70 decimal digits gives:

| \(X\) | divisor sum \(F(X)\) | \(\log(X!)\) | difference at 70-digit precision |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 15.104412573075515295225709329251070371882250744291936472188903343 | 15.104412573075515295225709329251070371882250744291936472188903343 | \(-1.4489087\times10^{-70}\) |
| 100 | 363.73937555556349014407999336965563802782392106288727472767944888 | 363.73937555556349014407999336965563802782392106288727472767944888 | \(4.6365077\times10^{-69}\) |
| 1000 | 5912.1281784881633488781308867254938824717457141813189089514875036 | 5912.1281784881633488781308867254938824717457141813189089514875036 | \(-2.2255237\times10^{-67}\) |

For \(X=2,H=4,r=0,s=1\), the direct Gram entry and its divisor-pair CRT expansion both equal
\[
7.3993803535748211491169092621841435655636438886340731483028477577
\]
to 65 displayed digits; the 2-by-2 Gram determinant is
\[
0.60735628116809375852761676017871646419796859661596301170841242121>0.
\]
This only verifies the exact finite algebra and positivity.

### 5. Source audit

The factorial identity follows solely from unique factorization and integer multiples. Gram positivity follows from an explicit square norm. The coupling is real but its algebraic type is wrong for the target: it produces \(\Lambda(d)\Lambda(e)\) pair weights, while \(Q\) contains a signed linear sum over \(\Lambda(n)\). No \(Q\)-positivity, zero information, or equivalent target assertion entered; neither was any target inequality proved.

### 6. Discrimination test

| world | result | exact reason |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Curves over finite fields | **Required Hasse reproduction fails.** | The object is built from ordinary integer divisibility and \(\sum_{d\mid n}\Lambda(d)=\log n\). Replacing this by a curve zeta function gives Frobenius power sums, not the factorial floor identity; the Gram has no degree pairing for \(a\pi-b\), so it does not derive \(|\alpha+\bar\alpha|\le2\sqrt q\). |
| (b) Ihara zeta of Ramanujan graphs | **No spectral-bound analogue.** | Divisibility congruences do not encode the Hashimoto operator or its nonbacktracking eigenvalue bound. |
| (c) Random Euler products with independent uniform phases | **Required nonvanishing reproduction fails.** | The Gram is deterministic and has no independent phase martingale. The separate standard random-Euler proof uses \(\sum_p p^{-2\sigma}<\infty\) and Bergman evaluation; those are not consequences of \(G_{r,s}\). |
| (d) Davenport–Heilbronn | **Fails I-MULT.** | Its coefficients do not provide the canonical Euler factors and von Mangoldt divisor function used in the identity. |
| (e) Beurling generalized primes with regular integer counts and bad prime counts | **Fails the canonical I-LATTICE construction; externalized Gram is nondiscriminating.** | Ordinary congruence classes and the factorial floor identity are absent. If arbitrary weights are supplied on an ordinary interval anyway, square-norm positivity remains true without regard to their zero geometry. |

Candidate 39A does not meet the mandatory true-toy reproof and false-world discrimination conjunction.

### 7. Verdict

**DEAD — below G0.** The exact CRT pair formula is proved, but the factorial total is \(\log(X!)\) and the positive Gram remains quadratic in \(\Lambda\), with no controlled bridge to the linear Weil samples. The required Hasse/random-Euler reproduction is absent, and generic Gram positivity does not discriminate zero locations.

### 8. New-idea check

The divisor identity and factorial sum are classical Chebyshev/floor identities; the archive already records floor-remainder analysis in Pass 28 and the Pascal-carry first-difference collapse in the scale-separation ledger. The shifted simultaneous-divisibility Gram is a rearrangement of standard CRT counting and a logarithmic sequence Gram, not a new mechanism established here. No novelty or priority claim is made.

### 9. Lesson

An exact multiplicative–lattice coupling is insufficient when its natural positive polarization squares the von Mangoldt divisor sum: the resulting pair correlation cannot be substituted for the target’s one-prime linear functional. The next attempt must obtain a signed linear prime sample from a lattice identity without inserting target values as constraints, and its true-toy proof must use that same transformation.

### 10. Seed

Seek a prime-linear lattice pairing—one whose exact expansion contains \(\Lambda(n)g(\log n)\) before any squaring—and prove its sign through the same pairing. Reject any proposal whose first PSD step changes the one-index prime trace into a two-index ratio or divisor-pair Gram.

## Pass 40 — CONSOLIDATION: exact finite support, milestones, and the unit-norm diagnostic

### 1. Milestone audit

The ledger records several G0 demonstrations: the finite-field Hasse degree-pairing toy proof (Pass 11A), and standard random-Euler nonvanishing/mean-square results in the random-phase setting (Passes 26A, 27A, 29A, and later reproductions). These establish the listed toy statements by their recorded mechanisms. They do not give a G1 arithmetic mechanism for the target form. No G1, G2, G3, or G4 result is recorded: there is no proved new unconditional target-sign theorem, dense target test family, or canonical Xi spectral identification.

Passes 36–39 sharpened where the constructions break. Pass 36's scalar entropy envelope optimizes away the actual CRT cylinder; Pass 37's primewise Gibbs relaxation leaves a positive limiting slack; Pass 38 removes that slack only by prescribing the exact CRT moments; Pass 39 genuinely couples the von Mangoldt divisor identity to integer CRT counts, but its Gram expands to Lambda(d)Lambda(e) pair weights rather than the Weil form's linear one-point Lambda(n) samples.

### 2. Most frequent death step and coupling audit

The most frequent single recorded death class remains false-world nondiscrimination: the death-step table counts 11 examples where positivity from generic positive measures, moments, or thermal traces survives in bad-zero generalized-prime systems. The recent passes also expose I-COUPLE failures more precisely. In Passes 36–39, the proposed arithmetic inputs either optimize away or prescribe the desired local data, or they produce the wrong algebraic output (a two-index pair correlation). This is progress in diagnosis, but not a replacement for the false-world test: all four recent candidates still fail to distinguish the stipulated bad-zero world, and Pass 40A's support fact is equally true there.

### 3. Three consolidation candidates

**40A — Finite ratio-lag support obstruction (developed).** For a finite set S of positive integers and coefficients a_m, define the ratio-lag distribution
\[
\nu_S=\sum_{m,n\in S} a_m\overline{a_n}\,\delta_{\log(n/m)}.
\]
Claim: no such finite distribution, even after adding pole and archimedean terms that are locally integrable away from the origin, equals the full Weil prime-power distribution.

**40B — Periodized ratio-lag Gram.** Reduce the logarithmic coordinate modulo a finite period and use the resulting finite Fourier matrix as a positive certificate. Claim: periodic aliasing turns the finite ratio set into the exact prime-power distribution. **Fast kill:** periodization identifies distinct real log lags modulo the period; a periodized finite ratio measure has only finitely many atoms on the circle and cannot recover the distinct unbounded real prime-power locations without an additional non-finite limit and a tail theorem. Any positivity of its finite Gram remains generic.

**40C — Finite prime-power Gram with a continuous completion.** Add a fixed continuous pole/gamma kernel to a Gram built from prime powers up to a finite cutoff and claim exact equality to the full Weil distribution. **Fast kill:** for every finite cutoff there is a prime p beyond it whose nonzero Weil atom at log p is absent from the finite Gram; a continuous distribution cannot supply that atom.

### 4. Develop 40A: scoped exact no-go theorem

Let S be a finite subset of the positive integers, let M=max S, and let
\[
\nu_S=\sum_{m,n\in S}a_m\overline{a_n}\delta_{\log(n/m)}.
\]
Since 1≤m,n≤M, every point in the support of \(\nu_S\) lies in \([-\log M,\log M]\). The Weil prime distribution has a nonzero atom at \(+\log p\) for every prime p, with coefficient of magnitude
\[
c_p=\frac{\log p}{\sqrt p}>0.
\]
Choose any prime p>M. Then \(\log p>\log M\), so \(\nu_S\) has no atom there. The pole term is a smooth function of the additive variable, and the archimedean term is locally integrable away from the origin; neither can cancel or create a Dirac mass at \(+\log p\). Testing against smooth bumps supported in a shrinking neighborhood of \(\log p\), disjoint from the finitely supported ratio measure and from other prime-power atoms, proves that exact distributional equality is impossible.

For the concrete support S={1,2,3}, the ratio lags are only \(0,\pm\log2,\pm\log3,\pm\log(3/2)\). The first omitted prime atom is at \(\log5\), with
\[
\frac{\log5}{\sqrt5}
=0.719762515553600491218663073278403452678895782385992138704959778207813619106
\]
at 75-digit working precision. This numerical value is illustrative; the proof is the exact support argument.

**Scope.** This proves that no single finite Dirichlet-polynomial ratio Gram can be the exact full prime-power distribution. It does not rule out a sequence of finite models converging distributionally, an infinite-support operator, inequalities rather than identities, or a remainder whose cutoff dependence is controlled uniformly. It therefore is not a positivity theorem and does not imply the target.

### 5. Requested Brownian Gram spectral-radius check

Use the Pass 15A definition
\[
K^{\rm Br}_{S,I}(p,q)=(pq)^{-1/4}\min(\log p,\log q),\qquad p,q\in I,
\]
with infinity a boundary label and not a matrix row. Recomputing the finite symmetric matrices with 75-digit arithmetic gives:

| S | eigenvalues | lambda_max |
|---|---|---:|
| {infinity,2} | 0.49012907173427359585695086181761669064573034954952736052112312353 | 0.49012907173427359585695086181761669064573034954952736052112312353 |
| {infinity,2,3} | 0.11349811770676396589537773730511468275814649398647183939761449411; 1.0109150546250736071479608970571510630871205574922563338796690081 | 1.0109150546250736071479608970571510630871205574922563338796690081 |
| {infinity,2,3,5} | 0.083714210778952372988637308436009284098042242634421574004894447417; 0.20074574836812490108858975854671514087172100117590989381106547029; 1.5597157287383607901847746406579447735543995900543888441662833628 | 1.5597157287383607901847746406579447735543995900543888441662833628 |

The one-prime block is below 1, but adding 3 takes the largest eigenvalue above 1, and adding 5 increases it further. Thus this family does not remain contractive with room to spare; the raw Brownian Gram fails the proposed unit-contraction filter already on {2,3}. These values are numerical diagnostics for this specified kernel, not an RH inference.

### 6. Source audit and five-world test

The explicit formula's prime-power atoms and their \(\Lambda(n)/\sqrt n\) weights are the standard Weil explicit-formula distribution; see [Bombieri, “Remarks on Weil’s quadratic functional in the theory of prime numbers, I”](https://www.bdim.eu/item?id=RLIN_2000_9_11_3_183_0). The finite-support conclusion itself uses only support of a finite sum of Dirac masses and uniqueness of distributions. The Brownian kernel definition is the earlier Pass 15A feature Gram; its positivity is an explicit \(L^2\) Gram identity. No zero locations or target positivity are used.

| world | result | reason |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Curves over finite fields | fails Hasse reproduction | No curve, degree pairing, or Frobenius action occurs in the ratio-support theorem. |
| (b) Ihara zeta / Ramanujan graphs | fails spectral-bound reproduction | No graph or nonbacktracking operator occurs. |
| (c) Random Euler products | fails same-mechanism nonvanishing reproduction | The finite ratio measure has no independent random phase martingale; the support proof is not the standard random-Euler proof. |
| (d) Davenport–Heilbronn | no canonical I-MULT input | The theorem begins with a finite integer Dirichlet polynomial, not canonical Euler data from this model. |
| (e) Beurling generalized primes with bad zeros | nondiscriminating | Any finite set of generalized-prime ratios is bounded in log support while the infinite generalized-prime distribution has further atoms; the same obstruction holds independently of zero locations. |

### 7. Scoped no-go status and verdict

**Proved, narrowly:** exact finite ratio-lag distributions cannot equal the complete infinite prime-power distribution. This refines the Pass 15A ratio-correlation diagnosis and the Pass 39A pair-correlation diagnosis by giving a support theorem for every finite S.

**Not proved:** any obstruction to cutoff limits, norm/weak convergence, a positive infinite operator, or an alternative non-ratio-lag construction. The false-world test and the required toy reproof both fail. Candidate 40A is **DEAD below G0** as a proposed exact finite realization; it contributes a rigorous class limitation, not a route to the target sign.

### 8. Novelty and selection-bias audit

Finite support of a finite Dirichlet polynomial's ratio set, local finiteness of prime-power log atoms, and uniqueness of distributions are standard. Pass 15A already recorded ratio-lag cross terms, and Pass 39A recorded the two-index divisor-pair mismatch; this pass makes their exact finite-support consequence explicit for the whole finite ratio-Gram class. No novel theorem or priority claim is made.

The search still selects candidates whose positive Gram is immediate. The finite-support no-go does not repair that bias; it only rules out claiming exact finite equality from a bounded lag set. The more productive shift is to require the signed one-point prime samples to appear before the positivity step, while keeping a separate proof obligation for false-world discrimination.

### 9. Consolidation decision and next seed

Passes 36–39 are increasingly dying at the coupling/output-type step (erased or prescribed local data, or pair weights instead of one-point samples), but false-world nondiscrimination remains present in each. The dominant historical death-step count is still the generic positive-measure/thermal-trace class, with 11 recorded examples. Do not say the protocol has solved the coupling problem.

**Pass 41 seed:** define a cutoff family of signed measures whose support grows and whose prime-power masses are exactly \(\Lambda(p^k)/p^{k/2}\); specify the topology and prove a uniform tail estimate before introducing a positive Gram factorization. Reproduce the same construction in the Hasse toy world and derive its Hasse bound from the same pairing; then test the exact construction in the random Euler and bad-zero Beurling worlds before claiming any sign.


The nine-operator rotation restarts with **TOY-FIRST** at Pass 41. Pass 40 is a consolidation, not a new operator pass.

## Pass 41 — TOY-FIRST: modular scattering exposes Xi zeros but does not locate them

### 1. Operator used

**TOY-FIRST.** Start from the classical modular Eisenstein scattering problem, where the integer lattice, Euler product, and a self-adjoint Laplacian already coexist. Test whether its exact scattering coefficient gives more than a meromorphic location for zeta zeros: the required toy check is that the same positivity-and-scattering argument reproduce an elliptic-curve Hasse bound or the random-Euler nonvanishing theorem.

### 2. Requested Brownian-kernel gate

Using K(p,q)=(pq)^(-1/4) min(log p,log q), with infinity a boundary label and not a matrix row, the recomputed largest eigenvalues are:

| S | lambda_max | 1-lambda_max |
|---|---:|---:|
| {infinity,2} | 0.490129071734273595856950861817616690645730349549527360521123 | 0.509870928265726404143049138182383309354269650450473 |
| {infinity,2,3} | 1.01091505462507360714796089705715106308712055749225633387967 | -0.0109150546250736071479608970571510630871205574922563 |
| {infinity,2,3,5} | 1.55971572873836079018477464065794477355439959005438884416628 | -0.559715728738360790184774640657944773554399590054389 |

The one-prime block is contractive; adding 3 already makes the norm exceed 1. This closes the requested below-1-with-room gate for this Brownian kernel, so I do not extend that kernel family here.

### 3. Generate three candidates

**41A — Modular Eisenstein scattering coefficient (developed).** On PSL₂(Z)\H, use the nonholomorphic Eisenstein series and its constant-term scattering coefficient
phi(s)=sqrt(pi) Gamma(s-1/2)/Gamma(s) · zeta(2s-1)/zeta(2s)=Lambda(2s-1)/Lambda(2s).
Claim: the positive Laplacian, exact Poisson/theta continuation, and scattering functional equation force every zero of the Xi-identified coefficient onto its spectral line. **I-MULT:** the Euler products of the two zeta factors in phi supply the prime-power channels. **I-LATTICE:** the Eisenstein sum over primitive integer pairs is continued by Poisson summation on Z², equivalently the theta transform. **I-COUPLE:** the same Fourier constant-term calculation turns that primitive lattice sum into the Euler quotient Lambda(2s-1)/Lambda(2s).

**41B — Theta–phase scattering transfer.** Let X_p be independent uniform phases and use the logarithm of product_p(1-X_p p^(-s))^(-1), with its variance bounded by the Jacobi theta Mellin identity. Claim: reinterpret the random boundary values as a positive scattering channel and recover nonvanishing in Re(s)>1/2. **I-MULT:** the logarithm expands over prime powers. **I-LATTICE:** Jacobi theta and its Mellin transform bound sum_p p^(-2sigma). **I-COUPLE:** the same prime increments whose variance is theta-bounded are the proposed scattering amplitudes.

**41C — Finite theta-completed Euler scattering determinant.** For primes p≤P, take the finite Euler factor Z_P(s)=product_(p≤P)(1-p^(-s))^(-1), and multiply by a finite theta-lattice correction obtained by truncating the Poisson formula. Claim: a cutoff-independent finite determinant can retain exact s↔1-s symmetry and converge to Xi. **I-MULT:** Z_P is the explicit finite Euler product. **I-LATTICE:** the correction is a truncated Jacobi theta/Poisson sum. **I-COUPLE:** the proposed correction must transform the same finite prime factor into its reflected factor without adding non-prime terms.

### 4. Fast kill

- **41B:** Kill as a restatement of Pass 26A: theta bounds only the random-phase second moment, and the known Bergman martingale proves the toy theorem; that argument does not produce a scattering operator or constrain deterministic zeros. External random phases can be attached to false Beurling systems, so the construction is not zero-selective.
- **41C:** Kill: Poisson summation is exact for the complete integer lattice, while Z_P(s) is a finite prime product. Truncating the lattice breaks the exact theta transformation; retaining the full transform adds terms that are not the finite Euler product. The cap/reflection and finite-transfer failures in Passes 22–25 already show that a finite symmetric completion does not identify the infinite determinant.

### 5. Develop 41A: exact scattering identity and the line mismatch

Let H={x+iy:y>0}, Gamma=PSL₂(Z), and Gamma_infinity the stabilizer of the cusp at infinity. For Re(s)>1, define
E(z,s)=1/2 sum_(c,d in Z², gcd(c,d)=1) y^s/|cz+d|^(2s).
Its constant Fourier term at the cusp is
E_0(y,s)=y^s+phi(s)y^(1-s),
where
phi(s)=sqrt(pi) Gamma(s-1/2)/Gamma(s) · zeta(2s-1)/zeta(2s)=Lambda(2s-1)/Lambda(2s).
Here Lambda(s)=pi^(-s/2)Gamma(s/2)zeta(s). Equivalently, for the entire completed function xi(s)=s(s-1)Lambda(s)/2,
phi(s)=s/(s-1) · xi(2s-1)/xi(2s).
The identity is obtained from the Fourier expansion of the primitive lattice sum; Poisson summation (or the Jacobi theta transform) supplies its continuation and reflection. It uses no zero locations.

The completed zeta functional equation Lambda(s)=Lambda(1-s) gives the exact scattering relation
phi(s)phi(1-s)=1.
For s=1/2+it, Schwarz reflection and Lambda(s)=Lambda(1-s) give
phi(1/2+it)=conjugate(Lambda(1+2it))/Lambda(1+2it), hence |phi(1/2+it)|=1.
Thus the scattering channel is unitary on its continuous-spectrum line.

Now let rho be any nontrivial zero of zeta, counted with multiplicity. Put s_rho=(rho+1)/2. Then the numerator of phi(s_rho) is Lambda(rho)=0, while its denominator is Lambda(rho+1)≠0: Re(rho+1)>1, where the Euler product is nonzero, and the gamma factor has no zeros or poles there. Consequently
phi(s_rho)=0, and Re(s_rho)=(1+Re(rho))/2.
So the zero data are visible in the scattering coefficient. The desired line for these scattering zeros is Re(s)=3/4; the self-adjoint Laplacian’s continuous spectrum is parameterized by the different line Re(s)=1/2. Unitarity on the latter line does not place zeros on the former.

The underlying Laplace–Beltrami operator has a nonnegative quadratic form,
inner_product(f,-Delta f)=integral_(Gamma\H)(|partial_x f|²+|partial_y f|²) dx dy ≥0
on its standard form domain, and its Friedrichs extension is self-adjoint. This positivity controls the L² spectral measure. It does not make the meromorphic scattering coefficient a positive operator-valued function, nor does it turn its zeros into L² eigenvalues.

An exact algebraic check shows why reciprocal symmetry plus boundary unitarity is insufficient by itself. For a=3/5, set S_a(s)=(s-a)/(1-a-s). Then S_a(s)S_a(1-s)=1, and |S_a(1/2+it)|=1 for every real t, but S_a has a zero at s=3/5, not on Re(s)=3/4. This is not claimed to be a modular scattering coefficient; it proves only that those two scalar identities alone cannot supply the missing zero-location implication.

Evaluating the exact modular scattering quotient with 80-digit working precision gives:

|---|---|---:|
| 1/2+i | 0.5231271516943812177286899312572318590994853014525250176 - 0.8522546468985216757820132864453408946748298930792904372 i | 1 |
| 3/4+i | 0.6584463851972562229872506167718359485214040870290172845 - 0.9845822218205726036732063490034619315798124199456336918 i | 1.184463504589511932098660832740463943815827630694692654 |
| 4/5+7i/10 | 0.5210898734638514341920060138221006656902668410948153617 - 1.320824642228726285248808918579639188740424282017485578j | 1.419898725876326536960226530071468525419043710708397927 |

The exact functional identity is phi(s)phi(1-s)=1; its numerical residual at s=4/5+7i/10 was below 10^(-80). The numerical values are checks of the formula, not proofs of the analytic identities or zero locations.

### 6. Source audit

The positivity comes from the energy form of the self-adjoint Laplacian. The prime information comes from the Euler products in phi; the additive lattice enters through the primitive integer-pair Eisenstein series and Poisson summation; the exact coupling is the constant-term coefficient. These ingredients are genuinely present in one classical object, but the positive form and the Xi quotient govern different spectral data. No target positivity or zero location is assumed. Zagier’s treatment derives the Eisenstein functional equation from the theta/Poisson transform and records the zeta quotient in its Fourier expansion ([Eisenstein Series and the Riemann Zeta Function](https://people.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/zagier/files/scanned/EisensteinRiemannZeta/eisenstein-zeta-978-3-662-00734-1_10.pdf)). Lax–Phillips’ automorphic scattering analysis relates the pole locations of the scattering generator to the Riemann hypothesis rather than deriving them from Laplacian positivity alone ([A Scattering Theory for Automorphic Functions](https://www.numdam.org/article/SEDP_1973-1974____A21_0.pdf)).

### 7. Discrimination test

| Toy world | Result | Exact step |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Curves over finite fields | **Fails required Hasse reproduction** | A curve’s rational zeta numerator and its Frobenius eigenvalues are not produced by the modular Eisenstein scattering calculation. No degree-pairing inequality is obtained, so the same argument does not prove the Hasse bound. |
| (b) Ihara zeta of Ramanujan graphs | **No Ramanujan-bound reproduction** | The modular Laplacian has no graph adjacency or nonbacktracking operator; no graph spectral-radius bound follows. |
| (c) Random Euler products with independent phases | **Fails required nonvanishing reproduction** | The scattering coefficient has deterministic zeta Euler factors and no independent mean-zero phase martingale. The standard random-Euler convergence proof is a different mechanism. |
| (d) Davenport–Heilbronn | **Fails I-MULT** | The function has no canonical Euler product to supply the local factors zeta(2s-1)/zeta(2s); substituting it changes the object rather than deriving its scattering coefficient. |
| (e) Beurling systems with regular counts and bad zeros | **Fails canonical I-LATTICE and does not discriminate** | The primitive Z² lattice and its Jacobi/Poisson transform are specific to the modular quotient. A Beurling system has no canonical version of this lattice step. If one externally replaces the Euler factors by generalized-prime factors, the reciprocal quotient algebra is still compatible with off-line zeros; it supplies no contradiction. |

### 8. Verdict

**DEAD — below G0.** The pass proves an exact classical identification of zeta zeros with zeros of a modular scattering coefficient and verifies the reciprocal/unitary identities, but it neither reproves Hasse nor random-Euler nonvanishing by the same mechanism. Its precise failure is the separation between positivity of the self-adjoint L² Laplacian and the location of zeros of the meromorphic scattering coefficient. The requested Brownian kernel also fails its unit-contraction check at S={infinity,2,3}.

### 9. New-idea check

The Eisenstein scattering coefficient, its zeta quotient, its functional equation, and its relation to the continuous spectrum are classical. The toy scalar S_a is an exact counterexample to the implication “reciprocal symmetry plus boundary unitarity forces zeros onto a prescribed interior line”; it is not a new automorphic result. This is a new-to-this-ledger test of a concrete object, not a new theorem or priority claim. The Brownian eigenvalue check reproduces Passes 15A and 40A.

### 10. Lesson

A natural spectral object can put Xi-zeros into scattering data while a positive self-adjoint Laplacian sits behind the construction; that coexistence alone does not couple the Laplacian’s positive quadratic form to the scattering zeros. A next candidate must exhibit that missing positive boundary form explicitly and make its function-field analogue prove a Frobenius bound by the same inequality.

### 11. Seed

Derive a positive boundary pairing for an arithmetic scattering operator whose exact determinant is Xi itself, and whose function-field analogue proves the Frobenius bound by that same pairing. First test whether positivity acts on the scattering coefficient’s zero data, rather than on a separate self-adjoint interior operator; reject reciprocal symmetry and boundary unitarity alone.
